Name Estate Name Concern Ivliskova Abreptoro Education, orphanages Nobriskov Cubratdis Religion Nuikin Esecklae Academia, athletics Ocrotire Sanctesalat Fishing, woodwork Olzanik Kinisaradia Metalwork Piechota Aliesetti Ranching Pretorius Mundorhova Gambling Ritter Vetistiqua Fashion Tatenna Fulchighora Banking SUPERNATURAL INTRIGUE Magic and the supernatural heighten the gilded depravities of Borca. When planning adventures in Borca, consider including the following elements. Curses and Poison. Chapter 3 of the Dungeon Master's Guide describes curses, magical contagions, and poison. Villains in Borca might use these or adjusted versions of your own design-perhaps with signature cosmetic effects. Monsters. Monsters can serve as threats but also agents of noble houses. Shape-shifting creatures such as Imps and Wererats excel at infiltration, while Doppelgangers and Succubi might pose as nobles, innocents, or trusted allies. Creatures like Ghosts and Intellect Devourers might control individuals, making them behave in unusual ways, Choose a single monster, and consider how you might make it central to a devious noble's plot. ‘Spells and Magic items. Don't try to account for or incorporate all magic into adventures involving supernatural intrigue. Choose a single spell— such as Clairvoyance, Geas, or Speak with Dead~and consider how to make it central to a plot involving secrets and mysterious revelations. Consider doing the same with a magic item such as a Cloak of the Bat, a Hag Eye, or Sending Stones. Characters should earn rewards for using their own magic in clever ways. Borca CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Borca typically involve the domain's noble families. A campaign here might follow this outline Levels 1-3. A scheming noble targets the characters, presenting them with an opportunity or exorbitant compensation for a simple task. In the aftermath, the characters discover they have given up something of great value or realize they look like criminals. Levels 4~6. The scheming noble or a rival offers the characters a way out of their predicament, but this involves a dangerous confrontation or daring infiltration. Supernatural forces—such as jilted doppelgangers, cursed family members, or dead nobility~might aid the characters. Against expectations, the characters complete their mission and turn the tables on their foe. Levels 7-8. The characters’ activities attract the attention of Ivana Boritsi or Ivan Dilisnya, The Darklord invites the characters to their estate or corresponds with them from afar, using them as agents in their rivalry. For example, either Darklord might task the characters with seeking out ‘the missing will of lvana's father, Klaus. LeTHAL LAVENDER Adventure for Level 5 Characters Situation, The human siblings Eroso and Siobhan Manduchi (Medium, Neutral Evil Nobles) are scions of a minor noble family who stole a rare plant, not realizing it belonged to the Boritsis. After discovering the owner, they feign their deaths to avoid reprisal. Hook. Eroso and Siobhan seek out the characters to use as patsies in their staged murder. ENCOUNTERS The adventure consists of these encounters. Dig Your Vibe. Eroso and Siobhan Manduchi approach the characters and invite them to go carousing if the adventurers agree to share tales of their exploits. The two are Friendly and flattering. Exquisite Corpse. Soon after the characters leave the siblings, they hear a scream. If they investigate, they find Eroso's stabbed corpse surrounded by guards. Siobhan accuses the characters of killing her brother. A group of four Guards and one Guard Captain (in the Manduchis’ employ) moves to kill the characters, while Siobhan vanishes into the night. Wanted. More guards hunt for the characters but are easy to avoid. Before the characters flee the community, Nostalia Romaine (Medium, Lawful Evil Assassin), who appears to be human, discovers them. She explains that she works for the Boritsi family, shares the details in "Situation” above, and insists the characters help her find the Manduchis.

Page 97

Dead Walking. Any character who spends a day searching for the Manduchis and succeeds on a DC 14 Wisdom (Insight) or Charisma (Persuasion) check learns Eroso’s or Siobhan's whereabouts. If they investigate, they find a public site—like a theater or bar—where the noble is behaving recklessly. These aren't the actual Manduchis, but Waxworks (see chapter 5) striving to destroy themselves in public. The characters encounter at least two of these creatures, perhaps more at your discretion. Flawed Perfection, After the characters encounter at least two self-destructive Manduchi waxworks, they find a Waxwork convinced that it is the new, improved Eroso Manduchi, and it doesn't want to die. If the characters promise to let it go, the Construct leads them to Lethal Lavender, the secret club where the Manduchis are hiding. Lethal Lavender. The interior of the Lethal Lavender club is overgrown with bruise-colored fungus. A pair of fungus-covered Zombie Plague Spreaders haunts the bar. In the VIP lounge, the plant the Manduchis stole has bloomed into a Violet Fungus Necrohulk composed of the real Manduchis and their remaining wax duplicates. Jvana’s Favor. After the monster's defeat, Nostalia finds the characters. She rewards them with a vial of Boritsi perfume worth 2,000 GP and a letter from Ivana Boritsi inviting them to a private social event at Misericordia in a week's time, Each character also earns a bonus 1,100 XP. DARKON Domain on the Brink of Destruction Darklord: None Genres: Dark fantasy and disaster horror Hallmarks: Fractured realm, magical ruins, ongoing supernatural catastrophe Mist Talismans: Ashes of a corpse, coin stamped with Azalin Rex's face, tainted spring water Aligned Tarokka Card: Necromancer Opposed Tarokka Card: Donjon a SNeccomaneee Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow Darkon is a sprawling realm of somber gothic cities, vast wildernesses, hidden secrets, and the dead. The ruins of forgotten magical empires scar the land, Undead creatures haunt the domain's lonely reaches, and the living acknowledge that the land's morbid magic might one day reanimate their corpses. Yet, for all its mysteries, the domain of Darkon has failed, Across the realm, the Mists close in, inexorably overwhelming the land. These Mists tumed ravenous are known as the Shroud, and what they consume doesn't reemerge. Darkon's dissolution ties to the disappearance of its Darklord, Azalin Rex. During a disastrous event known as the Hour of Ascension, Azalin's citadel, Castle Avernus, was destroyed, and a mysterious golden star called the King's Tear appeared in the heavens, Since then, no one has seen Azalin, and the Shroud has eroded the domain. Despite the encroaching annihilation, life in Darkon hasn't changed. The residents of towns and cities far from the domain's borders dismiss reports of vanishing frontiers as fearmongering. As Darkon splits into crumbling islands, ambitious individuals vie for Azalin's power, each touting that they alone can save the land by becoming its tyrant.

Page 98

DOMAIN FEATURES Darkon is known for the following features: Divided Domain. The Mists consuming Darkon have divided the land into four separate regions. Hour of Ascension. Azalin Rex vanished during an event his servants call the Hour of Ascension. Inevitable Undeath. The night after any Humanoid dies, its corpse reanimates and shambles into the night. Locals swiftly burn bodies to prevent this. King’s Tear. Since the Hour of Ascension, a golden star called the King's Tear hangs in the sky. The sun and moon pass behind this star in their orbits. Secret Police. The Kargat, the domain's secret police, remains active in Darkonis largest cities: II Aluk and Martira Bay. LOCATIONS IN DARKON Darkon Is a place of dark wonders and apathy. The ruins of impossible structures litter the landscape, but the domain's jaded people ignore these marvels to focus on daily concerns The regions of Darkon are described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Darkon Locations table. REGIONS OF DARKON The Shroud has divided Darkon into four parts (see "Fractures of Darkon’ for details): Jagged Coast, Dense forests and shattered cliffs compose the Jagged Coast, skirting the Sea of Souls, Undead within the forests prey on isolated communities here, Lychgate. Lychgate is ruled by the dead. Aimless undead creatures wander the forest, milling around amid the time-locked ruins of Castle Avernus and many of Azalin’s powerful servants. Mistlands, Scattered communities dot the Mistlands, which were once Darkon’s wild eastern reaches. Strange monsters emerge from the forests and mountains to prey on the isolated lands. Rexcrown, The heart of Darkon, Rexcrown has become a sagging bog, From it rise the spires and grim wonders of Il Aluk, Darkor's capital, and the homes of the domain's indifferent elite. Map 2.5: Darkon Player Version # Damien Mammoliti Damien Mammoliti Darkon Locations Location Description The Brautslava Institute hosts scholars of varied fields, from science and the arts to pseudoscience and fringe Brautslava Institute ‘i ‘supernaturalism. The college of ethics is woefully underfunded. The castle of Azalin Rex was known as a place of grim justice and mysterious magic. Now it stands locked in time, Castle Avernus frozen in a moment of magical catastrophe. Most of Enge!'s End's residents have fled the Shroud, but a golden light shines from the belfry of the hamlet's chapel. AZALIN REX Darklord Who Won't Be Caged Guilherme Motta “itis customary to kneel before your dread lord." — Azalin Rex In Darkon, Azalin was a tyrannical wizard-king obeyed by the living and the dead. When he first appeared before his people, he adopted an illusion of the form he possessed in life: that of a middle-aged human man with dark hair and a superior demeanor. However, his true form was that of a moldering lich, eyes ablaze with blasphemous energy.

Page 100

Since arriving in the Domains of Dread, Azalin has been unable to learn new magic. For a wizard who forsook his mortality to pursue arcane studies, this was the ultimate frustration. Despite the limits on his studies, Azalin numbered among the few Darklords who understood the mysterious nature of the Domains of Dread. For generations, Azalin struggled to escape Darkon. His magical atrocities reshaped Darkon and the Domains of Dread time and again. However, these struggles gave Azalin only a glimpse of freedom, and his metaphysical violations inevitably reverted to the Dark Powers’ original designs. Nevertheless, Azalin refused to stay a prisoner. His most recent plot culminated in the Hour of Ascension and the destruction of Castle Avernus. Since then, no one in Darkon has seen the Darklord, and his realm has begun deteriorating. The stat block in this section presents the Darklord as he was prior to his disappearance and how he would be should he return. Azalin's current spirit jar is a golden amulet he carries. If this amulet is destroyed, Azalin’s original spirit jar—an immovable dragon skull in the ruins of Castle Avernus—reactivates, and the lich’s spirit returns to Darkon if his body is destroyed.

REGIONAL EFFECTS Darkon is altered by Azalin's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Azalin can close or open the borders to Darkon at will. While the borders are closed, undead terrors fill the Mists surrounding Darkon. Whenever a creature enters the Mists, one Hostile Zombie Clot appears in a random unoccupied space within 30 feet of that creature. Lord of the Dead. While in his domain, Azalin can cast Clairvoyance, requiring no spell components and using the same spellcasting ability as his Spelicasting action. When Azalin casts this spell in this way, he can place the sensor in any Undead in Darkon with an Intelligence of 6 or less, even if itis beyond 1 mile of Azalin. In addition, Azalin can speak through the Undead he placed the sensor in. No Secrets. Whenever a creature in Darkon casts a spell using a level 5+ spell slot, Azalin becomes aware of that creature's name and location. A Nondetection spell blocks this effect. If Azalin dies, these effects end immediately. AZALIN'S TALE Firan Zal'honan was a noble and heir to the city-state of Knurl on the world of Oerth. A student of magic, Firan adopted the title "AzaLan’ meaning *wizard-king’ when he came to power. Firan's rule was marked by harsh laws and violent expansion, but his people flourished, as their lives were filled with magical wonders. As Firan aged, he looked toward his succession. His son, Irik, was generous and charming but, in Firan's eyes, soft and unfit to rule, No matter how Firan tried to teach his son of the world's harsh realities, Irik’s kind spirit wouldn't bend to his father’s cynical outlook. This difference came to a head when Firan caught Irik freeing political prisoners, Firan could have ignored the crime or pardoned his son. Instead, Firan personally wielded the axe during Irik’s execution, Inthe years that followed, Firan was tormented by his son's death, but he also became convinced that only he was fit to rule. Researching the mysteries of death, he discovered the secret to becoming a lich. After embracing the transition into undeath, Firan ruled for decades more, increasing Knurl's power and earning himself many foes. Eventually, tales of magic that might restore Irik lured Firan from his home. During his search, a band of mercenaries ambushed the ruler. Firan fled into the mist to avoid the assassins, When the mist cleared, Firan found himself in Barovia In Barovia, peasants Firan encountered mistook his title and called him Azalin. Soon, Azalin encountered Barovia's ruler, Strahd von Zarovich. The two came to respect each other, and Azalin taught Strahd much of the way of magic. Together, the pair learned a great deal about the nature of the Domains of Dread and made attempts to escape. Their plots failed, though, and resulted in other realms—such as the domain of Mordent-being drawn into Barovia's orbit. Eventually, the two tyrants' respect soured into loathing, Before Azalin and Strahd could destroy each other, Azalin entered the Mists, and the domain of Darkon spread before him AZALIN'S DOMAIN Darkon is altered by Azalin's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Azalin can close or open the borders to Darkon at will, While the borders are closed, undead terrors fill the Mists surrounding Darkon. Whenever a creature enters the Mists, one Hostile Zombie Clot appears in a random unoccupied space within 30 feet of that creature. Lord of the Dead, While in his domain, Azalin can cast Clairvoyance, requiring no spell components and using the same spellcasting ability as his Spelicasting action. When Azalin casts this spell in this way, he can place the sensor in any Undead in Darkon with an intelligence of 6 or less, even if it is beyond 1 mile of Azalin. In addition, Azalin can speak through the Undead he placed the sensor in. No Secrets. Whenever a creature in Darkon casts a spell using a level 5+ spell slot, Azalin becomes aware of that creature's name and location. A Nondetection spell blocks this effect. If Azalin dies, these effects end immediately. AZALIN'S LAIR During the Hour of Ascension, Castle Avernus—Azalin's seat of rule and magical laboratory—erupted in a magical conflagration. Now, Castle Avernus stands frozen in time at the instant of its destruction, blasted to magically floating chunks of rubble. Its towers pitch wildly and crumble to pieces amid arcane flames, yet they don't fall. Although time has halted in the ruins, Azalin’s servants who avoided the disaster have returned, unaffected by the castle's frozen time. Meanwhile, the fortress's magic attempts to heal the wreckage, slowly creating new halls and winding stairs between the fractured floors. CASTLE AVERNUS Amid its magical destruction, Castle Avernus is a vast structure filled with Azalin’s arcane creations and summoned servants. Prominent locations from this ruin are depicted on map 2.7 and detailed here:

Page 104

Outer Bailey and Gallery. The castle's entry, called the Maw by Azalin’s servants, remains partially intact. Undead from the surrounding wilds lurk here, waiting for their lord's commands. Hall of Records. A column of stationary but still deadly magical fire blasts through Avernus's library. Scattered on ruined islands of rubble are the remains of a collection said to hold the biographies of all who have died in Darkon. ‘Tomb of irik Zalhonan. Here stands the tomb of Azalin's son, Irik, The Ghost of Irik appears to those with benevolent intentions, Despite his tragic fate, he forgives his father—but he doesn't want Azalin to harm anyone else. He can't leave the crypt and knows little of Azalin's plots. Lair of Ebbasheyth. The Shadow Dragon Ebbasheyth served as Azalin's adviser and steed. While she has moved much of her hoard to Mount Nyid, she still watches over the treasures here. Hall of Life. The Hall of Life has largely crumbled and is accessible only via gaps in the celling of the floor below. Within survives a circle of glass and emerald cylinders. These hold the remains of Azalin’s magical cloning experiments. A gigantic dragon skull made of gold has fallen into the midst of these failed experiments. This is Azalin’s original spirit jar, but the skull’s eyes, which once glowed with unholy flames, now look empty. Heart of Avernus. Amid the tower's wreckage glows the magical anomaly called the Heart of Avernus. This is the epicenter of the disaster that destroyed the castle during the Hour of Ascension. This mysterious force draws droves of the undead to feed on the ongoing magical reaction. FRACTURES OF DARKON The domain of Darkon has fractured into four regions: the Jagged Coast, Lychgate, the Mistlands, and Rexcrown. In Azalin’s absence, ambitious villains unleash terrors as they strive to dominate their piece of the domain. This section explores the horrors unfolding in each region of Darkon. JAGGED COAST Stark vistas of sheer cliffs, mysterious islands, and knotted forests bleed from one to the next along the Jagged Coast. The shadow of Castle Avernus once loomed over the southern part of the region, enforcing grim peace on the small communities there, In the north, the thriving city of Martira Bay prides itself on its inhabitants’ wealth and reason Since Azalin's disappearance, the Darklord's secret police have maintained order here—obeying not the commands of the missing king, but rather the will of their regional leader, Alcio Metus.

Page 105

The Treacherous Harbor of Martira Bay Titus Lunter KAaRGAT Azalin's secret police, the Kargat, has long enforced the Darklord's edicts and smothered any ember of rebellion in Darkon. Those “disappeared! by the Kargat are never seen again. While all Darkon fears these dark-cloaked spies, few realize the Kargat is largely composed of vampires and other shape-shifting creatures. Members operate in cells assigned to particular tasks or locations, with each reporting to a regional leader. The highest-ranking members reported to Azalin. Serving the Kargat are the Kargatane, a cultlike rank of living informants and initiates who spy on their neighbors in hopes of earning supernatural power and induction into the larger organization. ‘The Hour of Ascension threw the Kargat into disorder. Taking advantage of this opportunity, Alcio Metus (Medium, Neutral Evil Vampire), a Kargat leader and criminal kingpin in Martira Bay, slew her peers and took over the western branch of the group. From the Black Tower in Martira Bay, Alcio spreads her Kargat spies across region and beyond. Martira Bay ‘A foggy port on the Sea of Souls, Martira Bay is a cosmopolitan hub of trade and ideas. The populace prides itself on its worldliness and believes that the best of Darkon and lands beyond passes through the city. Martira Bay's people appreciate reason and tangibility, and many have never left the city. This makes many residents incredulous of magic and the supernatural, even as séances and spiritualist practices flourish in posh salons. Martira Bay is infamous for sensationalist murders. Newspapers such as The Bullseye update their devoted readership on the grisly details of sprees committed by killers such as the Midnight Slasher, the Spider, and the Weeping Woman. The overwhelmed Martira Bay constabulary works to counter these killers, as do professional detective agencies including the Ray Detectives (founded by Alanik Ray; see chapter 3) and the Dusklight Detectives.

Page 106

Map 2.6: Martira Bay Player Version & Jared Blando # Jared Blando LYCHGATE Lychgate hides the magically shattered remains of Castle Avernus, Whatever ongoing magical disaster befell the castle during the Hour of Ascension has locked its ruins in time, freezing it in a semi-exploded state. While Castle Avernus remains the lair of some of Azalin's most dangerous servants, the surrounding wilderness has become overrun by shuffling corpses and mournful spirits drawn to the castle's strange magic. The region is ruled by Ebbasheyth, Azalin's Shadow Dragon ally who maintains a lair in Castle Avernus. ‘Adventures in Lychgate likely relate to the mystery of Azalin's disappearance. Roll on or choose a result from the Doom in Lychgate table to inspire adventures in this ruined realm, Doom in Lychgate 1d6 In Lychgate, Adventurers Might Seek... 1 Azalin’s spirit jar or other magical treasure. The corpse of an ally whose body rose as a zombie and wandered into the Mists. ‘A Darklord's treasure held in Avernus. Details of Azalin’s fate. Duplicates of Azalin, an ally, or themselves. aaron A passage to the King's Tear. MISTLANDS Even before the Hour of Ascension, western Darkon was a land of magical mysteries and strange creatures. No longer fearing the Darklord’s magic, ancient evils have crept from the shadows to terrorize and control the scattered communities, in the town of Nevuchar Springs, an elf mystic and leader of the Eternal Order, Cardinna Artazas, works to turn back the Shroud, To do this, she sought to resurrect a being bonded to the land and capable of bending the Mists to its will, This resulted in the corruption of her order’s sacred pools and unleashed Darcalus Rex, a Necrichor (see chapter 5) that claims to be the first king of Darkon, Uncertain of what it has unleashed but desperate for salvation, the Eternal Order serves the whims of Darcalus Rex as the strange being grows in power. REXCROWN Residents of Rexcrown consider themselves the only true Darkonians due to their proximity to the capital, !! Aluk. Despite that prestige, Rexcrown is covered in deadly swamps, depleted mountains, and fields that produce disturbingly malformed crops. The city of Il Aluk is built in a bend in the Vuchar River and rises in countless gothic spires. These mark the homes of the city's elite, severe governmental buildings, and temples dedicated to dozens of grim deities. Serving the city are the vassal villages of Decimus, Desolatus, and Despondia, where peasants struggle to feed the city’s people and the decadent lifestyles of local nobles. As the Shroud spreads fear among traders, II Aluk suffers from shortages in food and other supplies. The city's residents have grown desperate and resentful of the exploitative upper class. This has resulted in violence and the ransacking of wealthy homes. The city's elite have rallied behind one of their eldest members, Lady Talisveri Eris. Eris is a vain human who uses elaborate fashions and makeup to disguise the fact that her body is permanently imperceptible. Once each moon, she incites her fearful peers to go out at night and commit violence while using her high-priced Potions of Greater Invisibility. These magical attacks and Eris's pompous, detached rhetoric exacerbate tensions in the city while enriching her nearly bankrupt family. ADVENTURES IN DARKON Adventures set in Darkon's disconnected regions often involve the mystery of Azalin's disappearance, the domain's dissolution, or the metaphysical nature of the Domains of Dread. Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain. Horror IN DARKON Beyond the adventures you might develop in Darkon's disconnected regions, the following elements persist across the domain.

Page 107

AZALIN'S FATE The events of the Hour of Ascension and the fate of Azalin loom over adventures in Darkon. The details of these mysteries are yours to decide. You can take inspiration from the following possibilities when determining Azalin's fate. Severed Darkiord, During the Hour of Ascension, Azalin’s body was destroyed as he tore open portals to the Negative and Positive Energy Planes. The former portal lies at the heart of Castle Avernus, while the latter is known as the King's Tear. The opposed planar energies are disrupting Darkon. Once the domain is destroyed, Azalin theorizes, his spirit jar will be released into the Shadowfell. Shattered Darklord, The Hour of Ascension was Azalin' failed attempt to become one of the Dark Powers. His essence is now trapped within his spirit jar, scattered pieces of his iron crown, and the floating amber sarcophagus known as the King's Tear. Whoever frees Azalin’s essence will become the new Darklord, Unmoored Darklord. During the Hour of Ascension, Azalin split his soul, leaving a piece in Darkon while the greater part escaped through a metaphysical rent between domains—the King's Tear. Now, disguised as the scholar Firan Zal’honan, Azalin searches for secrets of the Dark Powers in other domains. He avoids entering the Mists without Mist-wandering guides, fearing the Dark Powers will find him otherwise. THE DEAD SERVE In Darkon, Humanoid corpses reanimate as Zombies 24 hours after death. These corpses obey Azalin. While he remains missing, they roam in a winding path toward Castle Avernus. These zombies avoid other creatures, but they fight anything that blocks their path. THe SHROUD During the day, one can traverse the Mists surrounding Darkon as normal, allowing travel between domains or regions of Darkon. At night, the Mists surrounding the domain turn deadly and encroach on the land—sometimes by infinitesimal degrees, other times in unstoppable floods. A. creature that touches the Shroud takes 7 (2d6) Necrotic damage and is Frightened until the end of its next turn. If a creature is slain by the Shroud or enters it entirely, the creature vanishes, What becomes of it is a mystery. DARKON CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Darkon likely involve the mystery of the Hour of Ascension. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-3, The characters arrive in Darkon near Il Aluk, Martira Bay, or Nevuchar Springs, They soon make an ally among those who oppose the villains rising to replace Azalin: Alcio Metus, Darcalus Rex, or Talisveri Eris. The characters help defend their allies and learn of these villains’ methods, Levels 4-15, The characters confront the region's villain, Rather than fighting the characters, the villain entreats the group to help them establish control over the domain, which they believe will reunite Darkon's regions and end the threat of the Shroud, To do so, they must defeat the domain's other villains or recover the scattered remnants of Azalin's power. The characters work to save Darkon while undermining their wicked patron, Levels 16-18, The characters’ adventures take them to Castle Avernus, where they must either claim a remnant of Azalin's might or learn his fate. In doing so, they can restore Azalin and bring his wrath down on those who would claim his throne. The threat of the Shroud and those who would claim Azalin’s throne has grown too great to ignore. An ally directs the characters to the only place they can find answers regarding Azalin’s disappearance: Castle Avernus,

Characters who mention their desire to restore Azalin have Advantage on this check. If made Indifferent, Ebbasheyth tells the characters the archives might hold details of Azalins schemes. if asked what name to search for, she doesn't know but relates that the spirit in the room below might. Conclusion. The biography of Firan Zal'honan in the archive reveals the secret of Azalin’s disappearance. From this, the characters should know how to find or restore Azalin or how to recover his power. Each character also earns a bonus 9,800 XP. DEMENTLIEU Domain of Decadent Delusion Darklord: Saidra d'Honaire Genres: Dark fantasy and psychological horror Hallmarks: Masquerades, decadent aristocracy, social decay, illusions, impostor syndrome Mist Talismans: Jeweled or feathered mask, article of well-worn fine clothing, shoe made of glass or gold Aligned Tarokka Card: Charlatan Opposed Tarokka Card: Seer

Page 109

# Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow Composed of the city of Port-a-Lucine and its shining, fog-shrouded outskirts, Dementlieu is a realm where the truth is smothered by collective delusion. Everything in Port-a-Lucine appears to be more than it is, and people behave as if the few examples of grandeur and prosperity were the norm, Beneath its facade, the city is rotten. Despite pervasive claims of happiness and success, the people of Dementlieu fight to survive and maintain social appearances. Every individual struggles to hide their struggles. Lying is central to life in the domain, creating a breeding ground for con artists and smiling villains. Behind the domain's denial of reality is its Darklord, the phantomilke Duchess Saidra d'Honaire. The cynosure of high society, this masked duchess ensnares the city's upper class in her exaggerated ideas of luxury and civility, During her weekly masquerade balls, Saidra's guests must perform to her expectations. Those who fail to charm her are decried as impostors and reduced to ash by her magic. Saidra’s terror also grips the city's desperate, Her murderous, unmasked form—the Red Death—stalks the benighted streets, preying on any who tarnish her preposterous vision of a perfect society. DOMAIN FEATURES Dementlieu is known for the following features: City of Lies. Nothing in Port-a-Lucine is as it appears. Everyone pretends to be more successful than they are. Lies great and small fill everyday communication. All residents know these truths, but none dares speak of them. Grand Masquerade. Every week Duchess d'Honaire holds a Grand Masquerade. Invitations are coveted, and invitees who don't attend rarely survive for long. Lord Governor. Administration of Port-a-Lucine rests in the hands of Lord Governor Marcel Guignol. He is counseled by aristocratic advisers, including the duchess, Masks. Decorative masks are common accessories, worn at social events and in daily life. LOCATIONS IN DEMENTLIEU Port-a-Lucine's grand structures and garish promenades dazzle viewers, distracting most from the grimy streets and cramped homes beyond. The countryside of Chateaufaux is described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Dementlieu Locations table. CCHATEAUFAUX The people of Port-a-Lucine speak of the countryside, Chateaufaux, where nobles summer at their estates and where idyllic villages send goods for sale into the city. But no one travels far beyond the city walls, no goods arrive from beyond, and Chateaufaux doesn't actually exist. The Mists creep close to Port-a-Lucine, occasionally exposing patches of forests and meadows but nothing more. Nevertheless, Chateaufaux remains a convenient explanation for whatever mysteriously appears or disappears.

Bonniville is said to be cursed. ‘The home of Saidra d'Honaire and the site of her Grand Masquerades is accessible only by the nobles’ stylish gondolas or larger ships (see "Saidra's Lair’). ‘The Great Library's supposedly peerless collection houses fewer than a thousand titles, but hundreds of copies of each. ‘A few rare tomes hide among the unremarkable copies. Alexandre du Cire's House of Wax displays wax copies of nobles and celebrities. In the basements, those whove been replaced by lifelike wax constructs remain imprisoned. The lord governor's home is the largest and most ornate in the city. The staff maintains only a portion of the estate, as the rest is said to be haunted by the city’s former rulers. The Ezra-worshipping clergy of the grandiose Mother of Tears Cathedral weep for all the suffering and horror of the world but take no action to cure or combat it. The seat of Port-a-Lucine's government is filled with empty council chambers. A few caretakers mind the gardens and the collections of art, civil records, and architectural plans. A patient with a preternatural ability to bend others to his will has taken over the Phlegethan Hospital. He controls its doctors and claims to be the rightful Duke d'Honaire. The opera is Port-a-Lucine's hub of culture and boasts ornate galleries, but the backstage areas are a labyrinth where crew members occasionally go missing— victims of fierce feuds. Assculpture of a giant crimson spider looms over the lively Red Widow Theater. Patrons seem undeterred by stories of shape-shifting spiders that hunt the theater's clientele. A coven of godmotherly Green Hags lives in three crooked houses on Mill Street. The hags fulfill their guests’ dreams, but their contracts always have a hidden catch. The University of Dementlieu is largely occupied by narcissistic nobles and their fatuous students. Some true scholars conduct actual research hidden from their clout-chasing peers.

SAIDRA D'HONAIRE Darklord Who Rules Illusions @ Lenka Simetkova “Welcome, treasured guests—you bright jewels in my city’s sterling crown. Bask in all you've earned and deserve. Oh... that's not a loose button, is it? ~ Saidra d'Honaire Duchess Saidra d'Honaire knows her life isn't her own—she knows she isn't alive at all—yet she ignores that for the sparkle of empty celebrations and a parody of her ideal life, The duchess terrorizes Dementtlieu, forcing the realm to conform to her desires. Those who expose the flaws in her fantasy and remind her of the desperation she once endured, she slays using her life-sapping power. All can plainly see Duchess d'Honaire isn't one of the living. Nevertheless, servants and sycophants flatter her poise, beauty, and array of lavish masquerade costumes. When not hosting decadent social events, Saidra removes her mask and slips into the night. Through the streets and alleys of Port-a-Lucine, the Darklord prowls as the Red Death: a murderous spirit that preys on liars, the struggling, and all who remind her of her humble origins. Saidra is overbearing and elitist, despite being unworldly. She fears being exposed as a fraud and projects this fear onto others, violently accusing them of being liars on the barest evidence or sign of vulgarity. Yet, in mysterious letters and rumors, Saidra receives evidence that her own simple, spiteful family dwells somewhere beyond her city. Their confessions could bring all her deceptions to light.

REGIONAL EFFECTS Dementlieu is altered by Saidra's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in her domain, Saidra can close or open the borders to Dementtlieu at will. While the borders are closed, the countryside called Chateaufaux appears in the Mists surrounding Dementlieu. Creatures in the Mists get lost amid mazelike hedgerows and fogbanks, only to reappear on the outskirts of Port-a-Lucine 2d6 hours later. Empowered Actors. Humanoids add 148 to Charisma (Deception or Performance) checks made while wearing a mask. Invitation. At dawn, Saidra can choose a creature whose name she knows or that she's seen before. A simple mask and an invitation to the next Grand Masquerade appear to that creature. The letter and mask appear in an unobserved but obvious space, such as a bedside table or among gear. If Saidra dies, these effects end immediately. The effects resume if Saidra gains a new body (see her Darklord Restoration trait). SAIDRA'S TALE Despite being raised in poverty, Saidra d'Honaire was an arrogant bully whose father led her to believe she was secretly royalty, When her father remarried, Saidra suffered slights and mockery at the hands of her callous stepmother and stepsisters. The night her father revealed that Saidra was only distantly related to nobility, she fled into the night. At her mother's grave, Saidra's curses were overheard by a mysterious stranger. This grandmotherly figure offered to grant Saidra any wish. When the stranger heard Saidra's desire, she cackled and dressed the girl in rubies and a crimson gown. Resplendent, Saidra set off to the masquerade ball celebrating the coronation of the land's new duke. At the masquerade, Saidra charmed the duke and his court. Yet, as bells tolled midnight, the gems on her dress evaporated, releasing a magical poison. The evil magic killed everyone at the masquerade, a massacre that would have fulfilled Saidra's murderous wish and left her within reach of the throne, As Saidra toured a palace full of the dying, clouds of spreading poison rose in a mysterious mist. When the mist cleared, Saidra found herself in a grand estate in Port-a-Lucine, The estate's servants acknowledged her as their duchess, but the lethal poison had dissolved Saidra's body into a deathly vapor. As none seemed to notice, Saidra embraced the fantasy that she had achieved her vain desires, SarDRa's DOMAIN Dementlieu is altered by Saidra’s presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. This lavish manor and its manicured grounds cover the largest island in Lucine Bay. D'Honaire Estate The d'Honaire Estate is well lit and filled with expensive decor and artwork—some of it quite good, but most is of middling quality. The estate is depicted on map 2.9 and has the following features: Grand Entry. A grand staircase sweeps from the estate's entry to airy balconies above. This broad space also serves as a ballroom and is the focus of the duchess's social events. Salons. Elegant rooms with distinct themes fill the estate. Popular spaces include the Skobeloff Salon (themed like a sunken ship), the Sulfur Salon (with dizzying wallpaper), and the Taupe Salon (filled with underwhelming taxidermies). ‘Sea Caves and Docks. Those who travel to the island estate by gondola dock in this sculpted sea cave. Stairs rise from the docks to the main estate. Second Floor. The halls remain locked and guarded. Within are the duchess's quarters, a joyless, hollow space that the Darklord haunts between stalkings and social events.

ADVENTURES IN DEMENTLIEU Adventures set in Dementlieu feature mystery and deception, often involving illusions and fairy-tale grandeur masking depravity and murder. Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain, The desperate elite of Port-a-Lucine gather: 5 ' Honaire’s islant for the Grand Me # Sylvain Sarraith Horror IN DEMENTLIEU Adventures in Dementlieu often include the following elements. Likes AND LIARS In Dementlieu, one can take little at face value. Terrible food might be called gourmet, while horrendous art might be dubbed avant garde, Those who resist such claims might find themselves mocked for not understanding a thing's appeal. This can lead to challenges that range from the comical to the surreal. Roll on or choose a result from the Dementlieu Deceptions table to inspire overlooked events that might lead to broader consequences. Dementlieu Deceptions 1d6 A Dementlieuse Liar... 1 Claims to personally know Duchess d'Honaire and so deserve special treatment. Insists they're investing all their resources in solving a problem they're clearly ignoring, Is publicly trapped or wounded but claims to passersby that they're fine. Passes off their rat-meat pies and gutter-water wine as food fads. Poses as an expert, but their advice is ludicrous or dangerous nonsense. aaron Says their stolen wares come from Chateaufaux. SCOURGE OF THE RED DEATH Between masquerades, Saidra d'Honaire fixates on her past and her fear that people suspect her ignoble origins. At these times, her glamorous facade fades, reducing her to a crimson spirit. In this guise, she slips into the night and scours Port-a-Lucine, targeting the sick, poor, or uncouth; those who disparage nobles; and those she suspects of investigating her past. The resulting life-drained bodies are attributed to a notorious figure locals call the Red Death, Using the Red Death, While Port-a-Lucine's residents ignore the Red Death's victims—potentially chiding corpses for their laziness or public drunkenness—the characters are unlikely to overlook bodies so clearly drained of life. Use such corpses to remind the group that the lies of Dementlieu's people are dangerous denials of reality.

Page 115

DEMENTLIEU CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Dementlieu often involve the discovery and unraveling of a conspiracy. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-3. Someone asks the characters in Port-a-Lucine for help finding a missing friend, opera performer, or noble. Following the clues, the characters learn this is but one of a rash of disappearances involving dangerous creatures hiding in plain sight—such as Gargoyles, Wererats, or Waxworks (see chapter 5). Level 4-5, The mystery points toward an elite member of society. The characters can't gain access to this individual, but they know the noble attends the Grand Masquerade of Duchess d'Honaire. The characters must prepare for and infiltrate the event (detailed below) to learn more. Levels 6-8. Learning that the suspicious noble supports or benefits from the disappearances, the characters investigate the noble's home or business (such as the opera house or House of Wax). There, they discover and disrupt the creatures behind the disappearances, likely finding that the vanished people have been turned into monsters. If the noble escapes, an unrefusable invitation to the Grand Masquerade might draw them out, resulting in a showdown during that event—and potentially attracting the ire of Duchess d'Honaire THE GRAND MASQUERADE Adventure for Level 4 Characters Situation, The Grand Masquerade is an opportunity for characters to meet Dementlieuse nobles and gain information they can't otherwise access. Hook. The date of the next masquerade is public knowledge. To attend, the characters need to create their own costumes and invitations. # Mike Schley # Mike Schley ENCOUNTERS, The adventure uses map 2.9 and consists of these encounters. Dressed to Kill, Attendees at the Grand Masquerade are expected to sport lavish costumes featuring fanciful masks, Any character not wearing an appropriate Costume, a mask, and at least 100 GP worth of jewelry (or appropriate magical alternatives) has Disadvantage on Charisma checks made during this adventure. Armor can't be worn with a nonmagical costume. If a character becomes Bloodied while wearing a nonmagical costume, their costume is destroyed. Arrival. At the entrance to the estate, servants check the characters’ invitations. A character with a forged invitation must succeed on a DC 10 Charisma (Deception) check to be admitted. Those who fail are asked to leave. If they refuse, four Ghouls wearing formal wear and masks fight them. ‘Masquerade. The Grand Masquerade unfolds in three tableaux. During each, the characters can decide to dance, gossip, flirt, or do something else. Each character can earn renown for their group with the ball’s attendees and host by making positive impressions on other guests (see chapter 3 of the Dungeon Master's Guide for details on renown). Each positive interaction can increase the group's Renown Score by 1 per character per tableau. Duchess d'Honaire has yet to appear, but hundreds of other costumed nobles mingle during the masquerade. These masked guests might share their names, but a character must succeed on a DC 10 Wisdom (Perception) check to identify even someone they're familiar with. The following guests pay particular attention to the characters: Alexandre du Cire (Medium Waxwor ee chapter 5) poses as a human and runs the House of Wax. He urges the characters to visit his establishment. His costume is a suit on one side and a ball gown on the other. He is Friendly. Angel Pajaro (Medium Werewolf) is a famed diva of the Port-a-Lucine opera and is Hostile. She likes to humiliate her fans but loves flattery from other performers. She's dressed as an arctic fox.

Page 116

Julienne d'Honaire (Medium, Neutral Good Bandit) is a Friendly human, She's bubbly and behaves in an unrefined manner. She claims to be related to the duchess but confides that she wasn't invited. Julienne wears a secondhand ballerina costume. Lord Governor Marcel Guignol (Medium Noble) is Indifferent and didn't want to attend the event. The elderly human is jittery and hates loud noises, and he seeks an excuse to leave. He's dressed as a smiling jester. Raoul Rameau (Medium Guard Captain) is a member of the city watch. He tries to guess who people are and why they've been invited—his hunches are poor, He's dressed as a toy monkey with cymbals. Uncouth Exchange. After the second tableau, two revelers wearing the same costumes as two nearby characters spot their lookalikes, Outraged by this faux pas, these Berserkers fight them, wielding sharpened candelabras (these function as Greataxes). If the characters defeat their attackers, their Renown Score increases by 2. The Duchess Appears. After three tableaux, Saidra d'Honaire (see earlier in this chapter) appears. She is fabulous and terrible as she drifts over the flattering crowd. Nearing the characters, she halts, gasps in revulsion, and points. "You! Never have | seen such an audaciously incompetent impostor! Who do you think you are to violate these hallowed halls?!" Near a character, Julienne d'Honaire begins to reply, and the duchess. instantly disintegrates her. A ghoulish servant soon appears with a dustpan to clean the spot where Julienne stood. If party members spent two or more tableaux with Julienne, the group's Renown Score decreases by 1. Conclusion. if the group’s Renown Score is 5 or less, the duchess shrieks, outraged to find more unworthies, Right away, four Ghoul servants appear and fight the characters; two Ghouls arrive every round until the party flees the estate. The duchess doesn't fight. If the characters’ Renown Score is at least 6, the duchess compliments one of their costumes as she drifts by in time with the music. If the characters came to the masquerade to meet or influence someone, that individual appears soon after, impressed by the duchess's compliment. Other members of the city's nobility or individuals the characters met earlier in the night also might approach them with invitations or offers of employment, Each character earns a bonus 600 XP. FALKOVNIA Domain Besieged by the Dead Darklord: Viadeska Drakov Genre: Disaster horror Hallmarks: Dwindling resources, impending disaster, suspicion, totalitarianism, zombies Mist Talismans: Correspondence from the capital, Lekar; gear marked with the Blood Falcon; zombie flesh Aligned Tarokka Card: Dictator Opposed Tarokka Card: Executioner ~ HENCCUtiONC gy, # Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow Falkovnia is a land besieged. Empty countryside surrounds ruined or crumbling cities. Only a few desperate pockets of civilization survive, carrying on not out of hope, but out of fear of the land's merciless soldiers. Led by General Viadeska Drakoy, Falkovnia's military organizes a desperate and occasionally effective defense against an implacable foe: the ever-growing armies of the dead.

Page 117

Every moon, a new zombie legion emerges from the Mists, Never appearing from the same place twice, the horde sweeps across the land, drawn to the densest populations of the living. That always includes the Falkovnian capital of Lekar, where unfit and underfed conscripts defend crumbling walls alongside General Drakov and her crimson-armored elite soldiers, the Talons. Causalities mount during the zombie sieges, but miracles and moments of desperate valor haven't abandoned Falkovnia. The people's numbers dwindle, but they soldier on. In the aftermath of a zombie attack, the Falkovnians burn their dead, repair what they can, and whisper that now might be the time to flee the city, Invariably, someone speaks too loudly, and soldiers drag off the so-called traitor to be impaled as an example. The people might want to abandon their homes, but Viadeska Drakov refuses to accept defeat. DOMAIN FEATURES Falkovnia is known for the following features: Army of the Dead, Every night of the new moon, thousands of ravenous zombies emerge from the Mists to feed on the living. Capital Punishment. There is only one punishment for any crime in Falkovnia: impalement. Rotten Remnants, Ruins cover Falkovnia—towns and villages crushed by zombie hordes and haunted by undead stragglers. Talon Soldiers. Falkovnian soldiers known as Talons patrol the land, hunting for deserters and strangers to press into service. Talon officers bear prominent tattoos of the Blood Falcon, the symbol of Viadeska Drakov. LOCATIONS IN FALKOVNIA Falkovnia is an unnervingly still domain, where people and animals hide to avoid attracting the attention of zombies and Drakov's brutal Talons. The city of Lekar is described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Falkovnia Locations table, LEKAR The only city of the living left in Falkovnia, Lekar bears the brunt of the zombie invasion. The city has outgrown its walls multiple times since its founding, leaving its districts divided by fortifications. During the first zombie siege, General Drakov sacrificed the northern River Ward, leaving hundreds of souls to be slaughtered but allowing the remaining defenders to turn back the zombie horde Now the city’s survivors face harsh conditions, Talon soldiers keep the peace in the cramped slums, Troops lead civilians outside the walls to scavenge from ruins and work the fields of abandoned farms, but their harvests are never enough for the city’s survivors. Though bitter and desperate, soldiers live better than civilians, assured of rations and housing in the Bastion Ward-the Talons-only district around General Drakov's command center at Custodian's Hall. From there, Drakov orders daily public executions as conscripts dispense supplies to the suffering masses. DRAKOV'S TALE In the world of her birth, Viadeska Drakov was known as the Crimson Falcon, leader of a peerless mercenary army called the Falcor's Talons. Ina land of bitterly feuding royal families, she and her troops executed a thousand ancient grudges and petty atrocities, Drakov amassed a fortune and planned to retire young and buy a title and a realm to rule, The sacking of Yeivere changed her plans. During that city's razing, Drakov’s mercenaries went too far. The Falcon's Talons killed a unique soul during the slaughter. None can say who the soul was—a royal, a saint, a witch, an angel—but with its death, the world tured against them. The ruling families united to hunt the Talons. Thanks to her tactical genius, Drakov repelled her pursuers and went on the offensive. One by one she attacked her former benefactors, burning villages, pressing citizens into service, and impaling anyone with a drop of noble blood. Over years of work, Drakov forged a bloody empire. But while sacking the burning city of her last remaining foe, Drakov and her troops were engulfed by smoke, When it cleared, everything they knew was gone. Transported to an unfamiliar land, Drakov and her troops wasted litte time subjugating it. This unprepared realm fell quickly, almost willingly. On taking the city of Lekar, Drakov named herself ruler of her new empire: Falkovnia. However, under a moonless sky, the dead rose against Drakoy, claws scraping against Lekar’s walls before alarms could be raised, Drakov's, brilliant and desperate strategies eventually turned back the zombies during the days-long First Siege of Lekar, but victory came at the cost of most of her veteran troops. Since then, Drakov has nearly lost her nation. Every moon, a new desperate battle unfolds. She prepares ceaselessly for each one, certain of her ultimate victory but doubtful of her troops’ mettle. With threats and public impalings, she keeps her people more terrified of her than of the undead, And with every new moon, she faces an unconquerable foe, never revealing a secret only she knows: that each zombie bears the face of an innocent her soldiers once put to the sword. Custodian's Hall rises over the few remaining wards of Lekar that haven't fallen to the zombie hordes of Miro Petrov Drakov's DOMAIN Falkovnia is altered by Drakov's presence, creating the following effects: Authoritarian Regime. Drakov's allies have Advantage on Wisdom saving throws. Closing the Borders, Drakov can't close or open the borders of her domain. Falkovnia's borders are usually closed, but they open one week every month starting on the night of the new moon. Endless War. Whenever a creature other than Drakov or her allies finishes a Long Rest in the domain, roll 120. On a 1, the creature doesn't remove any Exhaustion levels as a result of finishing that rest, and the creature gains 1 Exhaustion level If Drakov dies, these effects end immediately.

Drakov's LAIR When she conquered the city, Viadeska Drakov claimed Lekar's seat of governance, Custodian's Hall, as her army's headquarters. Since the First Siege of Lekar, one emergency after another has forced Drakov and her Talon troops to repurpose the building's chambers for military purposes. Map 2.11: Custodians Hall Player Version f Mike Schley f Mike Schley Custop1An's HALL The polished marble and hopeful mosaics of Custodian's Hall now bear the dirt and scars of invaders’ boots and tools of war. The site is depicted ‘on map 2.11 and has the following features: Chamber of Peers. The chamber of Lekar's dissolved senate now serves as a command center for Drakov's officers, Drakov's Chambers. Drakov has repurposed offices at the heights of Custodian’s Hall as her personal chambers. Her bedroom is cold and simple and features little more than a hard cot, Nearby, she maintains a war room full of maps and reports where she obsesses over desperate battle scenarios, Across the hall is a well-used chamber full of torture devices, many of her own design. Plaza Stairs. Daily, Talons disseminate meager rations to Lekar's citizens while other soldiers execute supposed traitors by impaling them on pikes. ADVENTURES IN FALKOVNIA Adventures set in Falkovnia involve zombie hordes, cruel soldiers, and Drakov herself, Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain. HORROR IN FALKOVNIA Adventures in Falkovnia often include the following elements. ZomBIE MARCH Zombies emerge from the Mists on the night of the new moon, This surge brings a recurring threat to besiege the walls of Lekar, but it also makes encountering droves of zombies more likely throughout the domain, Use the zombies from chapter 5, the Monster Manual or other sources to create surprising and terrifying encounters, THE FALcon's TALONS Drakov's military forces are both completely overwhelming and entirely outnumbered, Though relatively few in number, the Talons are organized enough to oppress Falkovnia's survivors and enforce Drakov's totalitarian laws. In the face of zombie hordes, though, the Talons are fighting a losing battle, Talon soldiers are Warrior Infantry, while officers are Warrior Veterans. MarrtiAL Law Lekar is a martial dictatorship. Drakov's troops carry out example-setting impalings as punishment for even the slightest crimes. These executions impose order through terror and make it clear that zombies aren't the realm's only monsters. ONLY Bab CHOICES There is one simple truth in Falkovnia: The dead are going to kill everyone. This sets the stage for countless bad decisions, from leaders making terrible sacrifices or permitting immoral acts to individuals making fatalistic choices. Locals behave as if it's their last day alive—because it probably is. TRAPPED WITH THE DEAD Falkovnia's borders are usually closed, and Viadeska Drakov has no control over when they open. Anyone who enters the Mists surrounding Falkovnia encounters endless numbers of zombies. Travelers who somehow avoid these shambling corpses emerge from the Mists back in Falkovnia, pursued by the undead. For one week following the night of the new moon, the Mists are empty of zombies and the borders open.

Page 122

During this time, Drakov sends patrols of Talons to sweep the edges of the Mists for deserters, These patrols are difficult to avoid, and battles with them often attract zombies. FALKOVNIA CAMPAIGN ARC Falkovnia is one of the few domains where the characters and the Darklord might be on the same side, united against unrelenting hordes of zombies. The characters might try to defend Falkovnia's people alongside Drakov, but as the Darklord's obsession with victory becomes clear, they must balance keeping her trust and upholding their principles. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-4. The characters save a group of commoners and outmatched Talons from Zombies. The thankful band takes the characters to Lekar, where people hail them as heroic "Trueborn of Falkovnia." Viadeska Drakov recruits them to bring scattered pockets of common folk to safety in Lekar. Levels 5-8. The characters witness the suffering in Lekar and see some of those they've saved impaled for disloyalty. Rebels entreat them to help smuggle people out of Lekar to hidden encampments in the mountains. Levels 9-12. The characters are captured by Talons, but Drakov offers them freedom if they defend the city's southern gate, the Gate of Torches, from the impending zombie siege. Unknown to the characters, Drakov plans to sacrifice the gate as detalled in the "Siege of Lekar' adventure. SIEGE OF LEKAR Adventure for Level 9 Characters Situation. Zombie hordes attack Lekar. Viadeska Drakov seeks to defeat them by sacrificing the characters and a district of Lekar. Hook. The characters are ordered by Drakov or entreated by citizens to defend the Gate of Torches. Lea Gare Uva Gare Map 2.12: Lekar Gate Player Version # Stacey Allan 8 Willam Doyle # Stacey Allan & William Doyle LEKAR GATES. This adventure takes place at a gate in Lekar's southern wall, the Gate of Torches, and a second gate to Lekar's central district, Loyalist's Gate. The Gate of Torches shows the scars of countless zombie claws, but otherwise the gates are identical. Both have the following features: Fortifications. The walls of Lekar are 30 feet tall, with the gatehouse's towers rising 40 feet. Crenelations atop both provide defenders with ThreeQuarters Cover from beyond the wall. Gates. The gatehouse's battle-scarred gates are Huge objects with AC 19, 72 HP, and Immunity to Poison and Psychic damage. Any character adjacent to the mechanism over the gate can open or close the gate in 1 round, ENCOUNTERS These encounters begin at the Gate of Torches, which uses map 2.12. Characters can position themselves where they please. Describe inexperienced soldiers guarding other posts or zombies beyond the map, but don't track their actions in battle. Wagon explodes (if not prevented). war aae Explosion (see “Siege Climax’). Zombie Attack. Zombies shamble from the south and attack the gatehouse's gates. Rather than having foes make individual attacks on the gates, assume the gates take 4d6 damage every time a round ends with a zombie adjacent to them, When reduced to 0 Hit Points, the gate is destroyed, If this occurs, troops flee as detailed in "Siege Climax.”

Drakov’s Talons, Starting on round 5, any character on or behind the walls who succeeds on a DC 12 Wisdom (Perception) check notices Talons (four Warrior Veterans) behind the gate. The Talons are supposedly allied troops, so this shouldn't cause concern. The Talons behave as detailed on the Siege Events by Round table. They flee if fought. Talon Wagon. This wagon contains kegs of gunpowder. A character adjacent to the wagon who spends a round investigating discovers these explosives. Drakov's Defeat, f Drakov is defeated, Falkovnia's borders open, and no zombies appear from the Mists for several moons. Some people rebuild, others flee into the Mists. After this time, though, Drakov reappears and slowly retakes control of Falkovnia. The borders close, and zombies return soon afterward Har'AKIR Domain of the Ancient Dead Darklord: Ankhtepot Genre: Dark fantasy Hallmarks: Ancient tombs, desert perils, lost gods, mummies Mist Talismans: Canopic jar, lapis lazuli scarab, scroll of hieroglyphics Aligned Tarokka Card: Monk Opposed Tarokka Card: Raven & Pam Wishbow Pam Wishbow Untold generations of tombs and monuments choke on the obsidian sands of the desert domain of Har'Akir. Here, past splendors lie forgotten, and the living bow in service to the mummified god-king, Ankhtepot.

Page 124

From a golden pyramid in the City of the Dead, Ankhtepot reigns as speaker for the gods and immortal pharaoh. The deathless ruler cares nothing for his domain or mortal concerns. Rather, he relentlessly sends servants in search of his one desire: his ka, the missing piece of his fractured soul As the pharaoh obsesses over his lost soul, his servants plague the domain in his name. In hidden tomb-courts, mummies known as the Children of Ankhtepot dominate cults as emissaries of mysterious gods. And in the mud-brick city of Muhar, Ankhtepot's priests oversee all aspects of life, apportioning food to the worthy and punishing blasphemers. But all the pharaoh's servants also pursue the Darklord's quest for his mysterious ka, though only his Children know the truth of this "treasure" he seeks. Each of them is desperate for some clue to placate Ankhtepot and spare them from his wrath. DOMAIN FEATURES Har Akir is known for the following features: Distant Pharaoh. The people of Har Akir know their ruler is an immortal who resides in the golden mountain called Pharaoh's Rest. Only priests can enter this massive pyramid. Extreme Environs. Intense heat and winds are common in Har'Akir (see the Dungeon Master's Guide), and water is scarce. Forgotten Ruins. The deserts of Har'Akir are littered with the remnants of a strange and glorious empire. The origins of these ruins remain a mystery. Mysterious Gods. Har'Akir's people worship a pantheon of false or silent gods. Manipulative or deluded priests oversee all aspects of society in these gods' and the pharaoh’s names. Preserved Dead. Akirrans mummify their dead so the pharaoh might call them to service, It is a crime to burn the dead. LOCATIONS IN Har'AKIR Har'Akir is dominated by treacherous, surreal deserts that obey Ankhtepot's will. The Labyrinth and city of Muhar are described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Har‘Akir Locations table, LABYRINTH Har/Akir's countless trap-laden ruins interconnect beneath the desert in a hidden maze called the Labyrinth. Monstrous Mummies and stranger creatures stalk these sprawling tunnels, Nevertheless, some priests use the few mapped sections to move about the domain unseen, MUHAR The city of Muhar is the only permanent settlement in Har'Akir. Here, pious people thank the gods for the water and food of the Muhar Oasis, From the city’s largest structure, the Temple of Ese, High Priest Isu Rehkotep speaks for Pharaoh Ankhtepot in all matters; her word is considered both royal decree and divine edict. Ankhtepot doesn't care how Isu runs Muhar so long as she maintains order and uses her influence to continue his unending search. Those who displease Isu or oppose her priest-quards are thrown into the Mouth of Oru, a pit that connects to the Labyrinth, EW Map 2.13: Har'Akir Player Version 7 Marco Bernardini 7 Marco Bemardini Har/Akir Locations Location Description su Bia This bent, black granite pyramid has no interior or obvious purpose. Some Akirrans claim it holds the magic of a forgotten god that one can manipulate using anclent music. Massive statues and the orate tombs of heroes and priests compose the City of the Dead. These crypts climb toward City of the Dead Ankhtepot's mountain-size golden pyramid, Pharaoh's Rest. The historian Kharafek has discovered numerous tombs at this excavation. @ Anna Podedworna “Bow before the pharaoh—whether as a servant or as dust.” = Ankhtepot ‘Ankhtepot has known treachery and conquest. He has known divinity and rule. But now he knows simply boredom and despair. He desires only to recover his lost ka, which he knows lies hidden in Har Akir. With it, he hopes to become mortal again, die, and face his gods’ judgment. Whether this means peace or oblivion matters not to him. Ankhtepot seeks only an end, and he and his followers will be a plague on Har'Akir until he finds it.

REGIONAL EFFECTS Har'Akir is altered by Ankhtepot's presence, creating the following effects: Blistering Sun. Any outdoor area of Bright Light in Har‘Akir is an area of extreme heat (see the Dungeon Master's Guide) Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Ankhtepot can close or open the borders to Har'Akir at will. While the borders are closed, whirling sands fill the Mists surrounding Har'Akir. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 2d6 Slashing damage. Control Undead. Undead of CR 5 or lower in Har’Akir have the Charmed condition and obey Ankhtepot's orders. If Ankhtepot dies, these effects end immediately.

Page 128

ANKHTEPOT'S TALE Ina nation called the Land of Reeds and Lotuses, Ankhtepot served the royal family as high priest. As old pharaohs died and new ones rose, the influential priest came to believe that the gods wanted a wiser leader to rule, On the day of a new pharaoh’s ascension, Ankhtepot rallied his priests and murdered their liege. But he had misjudged the people's will, and an outraged mob rose up and executed the traitorous priests. Moreover, Ankhtepot had misjudged the will of the gods. As he stood before them in death, the immortals forsook him, cursing him and denying him entry to the afterlife. They returned him to the world but stripped away a piece of his soul, his ka~the vital essence that inspires all living beings. Ankhtepot awoke, trapped within his corpse, as he was mummified along with his treacherous followers. Untold centuries passed, but on the day the last memory of Ankhtepot faded from the Land of Reeds and Lotuses, the high priest's bindings rotted away, and Ankhtepot crawled forth from his sarcophagus. He emerged from his tomb in an unfamiliar land: Har‘Akir. In this new land, Ankhtepot found a pious people devoted to the same gods he once served. Over generations, he wiped out that religion, replacing it with false divinities of his own imagining for whom he alone could speak. Ankhtepot also resurrected the priests buried alongside him as mummy lords, replacing their heads with those of beasts and monsters holy to his new faith. These Children of Ankhtepot served him as they did in life, and together the dead conquered the souls of Har’Akir. In the ages since, Ankhtepot hasn't forged the land of his ambitions. His servants scheme against him, his people dor't flourish, and his monuments crumble to dust in mere centuries. Ankhtepot's ambitions have withered. Now he seeks only to reclaim his soul and use it to put an end to his accursed immortality. ANKHTEPOT'S DOMAIN Har'Akir is altered by Ankhtepot's presence, creating the following effects: Blistering Sun. Any outdoor area of Bright Light in Har'Akir is an area of extreme heat (see the Dungeon Master's Guide). Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Ankhtepot can close or open the borders to Har Akir at will. While the borders are closed, whirling sands fill the Mists surrounding Har'Akir, A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 2d6 Slashing damage. Control Undead, Undead of CR 5 or lower in Har'Akir have the Charmed condition and obey Ankhtepot's orders. If Ankhtepot dies, these effects end immediately. ANKHTEPOT'S LAIR A mountain-size black pyramid, Ankhtepot's tomb—called Pharaoh's Rest—looms over the City of the Dead. It can be seen from all across HarAkir. PHaRAon's Rest FEATURES Ancient and ornate, Pharaoh's Rest holds countless carvings of mysterious deities and Ankhtepot as an idealized pharaoh. A labyrinth of trapped tombs, embalming rooms, and treasure chambers fills the majority of this massive pyramid, Broad paths used by Ankhtepot's servants lead past deadly side passages to the heights of the monument. Prominent locations are depicted on map 2.14 and have the following features: Ankhtepot's Tomb. The burial treasures and reliefs lining Ankhtepot's tomb's walls are shattered, having suffered ages of Ankhtepot's frustration. Hidden behind a bejeweled throne is a thoroughly defaced sarcophagus. Within, Ankhtepot awaits word of his ka's discovery, rising only when disturbed by trespassers or at the call of servants from the funeral barge chamber below. Funeral Barge. Ankhtepot envisions his trip to the afterlife taking place on a river. In an ornate ritual site, he has prepared a funeral barge and other ceremonial trappings necessary for his journey after his ka's recovery. Lesser Tombs. Throughout the pyramid are shrines, mummification chambers, and the crypts of Ankhtepot's servants. These include the burial chambers of several Children of Ankhtepot and the Darklord's son, Thutepot. Shrine of Ankhtepot. A vast, open shrine holds ominous statues of Har‘Akir's gods and columns etched with exaggerated and fictitious events from Ankhtepot's rule, On the night of the full moon, a hidden magical elevator activates, granting access to the tombs above. # Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow Hazlan is a domain drained of vitality, frayed by magical disasters, and overrun with abominations. Here the amoral, spellcasting elite have so exploited magic that it's turned erratic and dangerous to use. Encouraging these magical abuses is Hazlik, the domain's zulkir, a title meaning wizard-overlord, The Darklord postures as the domain's most powerful wizard and views all others as either apprentices or test subjects. All the while, Hazlik hides the fact that he's lost the ability to learn new magic. Paranoid and controlling, Hazlik constantly spies on those in his domain through the Eyes of Hazlik, These pervasive, eye-shaped symbols appear on structures, decorations, clothing, and individuals across the domain—and through these eyes, the Darklord watches. Hazlik survells his domain endlessly, reveling in the fear of his people, encouraging rivalries between his apprentices, and searching for magical innovations he can claim as his own, DOMAIN FEATURES Hazlan is known for the following features: Eye of Hazlik, inhabitants claim the eyelike design called the Eye of Haziik bears Hazlik’s blessing and wards off dangerous magic. In truth, the symbol is a sensor for Hazlik's magical surveillance. Magocracy. The wizard Hazlik rules Hazlan. His apprentices have free rein to exploit the land and its people to further their magical experiments. Shattered by Magic. The effects of magical disasters disfigure the domain, poisoning rivers with alchemical runoff, scarring the land with magical blasts, and unleashing unpredictable hazards Wild Magic, Magic is unreliable in Hazlan, resulting in dangerous side effects (see "Hazian Wild Magic”) LOCATIONS IN HAZLAN Magical disasters and supernatural environments make Hazlan's arid landscape even deadlier and its natural rock monuments even stranger. The settlements of Sly-Var and Toyalis are described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Hazlan Locations table. Sty-VAR In Sly-Var, mages obsessed with earning the Darklord's favor raise architecturally discordant towers that defy physical laws. The residents magically create whatever they need, resulting in little need for trade. This leaves the labyrinthine streets and knotted bridges between intertwined compounds eerily empty. The few common folk who traverse these surreal streets often disappear—the victims of magical guardians or of wizards who see the magically deficient as stock for their experiments. TOYALIS The largest town in Hazlan, Toyalis boasts a population of poor, hardworking commoners united by terror of the domain's magic-using elite. Colorful depictions of magical creatures, arcane symbols, and the Eye of Hazlik cover the town's rough wooden and adobe structures. Every few weeks, one of Hazlik’s apprentices leads a procession of attendants and magical wonders to Toyalis in search of replacement servants, new

apprentices, or stock for magical experiments. Hazlan Locations Location Balsam Wood The Brew Fallen Sisters Fleshless Forest The Lacuna The Marks Moonstone Valley Mound of the Worm Orbitoclasts Philosopher's Egg Gd Map 2.15: Hazlan Player Version @ Marco Bernardini # Marco Bernardini Description Aplant at the center of the dense Balsam Wood can cure any illness. Unnervingly, the forest is alive, aware, and prone to agony, screaming if someone disturbs so much as a leaf. Alchemical pollution from the village of Ramulai has poisoned the toxic marsh now known as the Brew. Creeks and bogs often erupt with violent fungal creatures and magical effects. The Fallen Sisters are three mountains that have collapsed into triangular, bottomless pits. Vistani often camp here. Plants and creatures in the Fleshless Forest were turned to stone. The condition is infectious. The Lacuna Is a region of knee-high mists that reach toward places of powerful magic, such as Hazlik’'s palace, Veneficus, Anyone who steps into these mists vanishes, and few reemerge. The sigils of Hazlik's mightiest apprentices mark the land in giant, magic-enhancing geoglyphs. The Lacuna grows toward these Marks—and a fourth, mysterious glyph. Meteors and otherworldly creatures crash into this wasteland during every new moon. Earth from deep underground rose to form the Mound of the Worm mesa, unleashing dozens of Purple Worms, The aged, albino worm Gravedrinker dominates this region. ‘The Orbitoclasts are floating rock formations that resemble lobotomy tools. Their pointed ends align toward the nearest magic-user. A cult of magic-warped beings worships an eight-story, citrine egg. The cult searches for a prophet who's never been touched by magic to release the antimagic entity within, ‘The Playas Former lakes, the Playas are now quicksand-riddled Purple Worm feeding grounds. Remulal Mines near the village of Ramulai yield rare magical elements. Refineries process these volatile resources, but in so amu doing, they magically sicken the people and the land. Seething The lifeless Seething region crumbles away into the Mists. A heavy footfall can break its surface. HAZLIK Darklord of Desperation and Magical Disaster

# Marianne Khali "You're new, | can tell. Impress me, and maybe Ill |et you be my newest apprentice.” —Hazlik The paranoid, egomaniacal archmage Hazlik clings to his power despite his magical ambitions being supernaturally smothered. Since Hazlik entered Hazlan, his magic has become unstable. Spells he casts behave erratically and fade swiftly~most lasting no more than an hour—while his, magical creations fall apart in about a day. The idea of others learning this terrifies the Darklord, which leads him to claim others’ works as his ‘own, spy on potential rivals, and order reckless research into the nature of magic and his domain Hazlik suffers vivid nightmares wherein past rivals taunt and torment him. The Darklord avoids sleeping, employing magical tinctures to stay awake, but this causes him to vacillate between bouts of exhaustion, jitters, and paranoia. Hazlik knows that his domain is being ripped apart by magical disasters—many of his own creation, He openly denies the realm's devastation, though, his fear of cascading ruin clashing with his inability to admit he's wrong
