REGIONAL EFFECTS Hazlan is altered by Hazlik's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Hazlik can close or open the borders to Hazlan at will. While the borders are closed, the Mists surrounding Hazlan are filled with monstrous magical experiments. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there must succeed on a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw or take 5 (1d10) Force damage and have the Restrained condition until the start of its next turn. A creature makes this save only once per turn. Frayed Magic. Whenever a creature (excluding Hazlik and his allies) casts a spell using a level 5+ spell slot, it rolls on the Wild Magic Surge table in the Player's Handbook to create a magical effect. Magic Nightmares. While in his lair, Veneficus, Hazlik can cast Clairvoyance, requiring no spell components and using the same spelicasting ability as his Spellcasting action. When the spell is cast this way, its range is all of Hazlan, Hazlik can see but can't hear through the sensor, and the sensor can appear only on an Eye of Hazlik If Hazlik dies, these effects end immediately. The effects resume if Hazlik gains a new body (see his Darklord Restoration trait) HAZLIK'S TALE On the world of Toril, Hazlik rose among the ranks of the merciless Red Wizards of Thay, Arrogant and convinced mortality was a weakness, he sought hidden truths of magic, ways to visit realms within the psyche, and command of reality. ‘As Hazlik’s power increased, he developed rivalries with other Red Wizards, many of which ended with his foes magically scarred, But one rival Hazlik couldn't defeat: the wizard Indreficus. Hazlik gradually came to obsess over Indreficus, viewing him as his only worthy opponent. Indreficus shared Hazlik's genius and arrogance, and they spent seasons as vicious adversaries and passionate lovers, They drove one another to feats of magical brilliance both revolutionary and unspeakable. This culminated when Hazlik discovered that Indreficus planned to betray Hazlik to garner the favor of Thay's ruling zulkirs. Heartbroken and enraged, Hazlik captured Indreficus and subjected him to nightmarish magical manipulations that permanently transformed him into a pain-wracked, living magical portal. Hazlik presented his creation to the zulkirs, hoping to gain their patronage The zulkirs revealed that Indreficus hadn't betrayed Hazlik. Rather, the ruling mages had watched the pait’s ascent and, wary of their ambitions, undermined them before they became threats. Moreover, they deemed Hazlik’s transformation of Indreficus an abomination. As punishment, the zulkirs decreed that every mage Hazlik had scarred could do the same in return to him, marking him with thelr personal symbols. As the first brand-wielding member of the mob of Hazlik’s rivals closed on the wizard, Hazlik fled through the groaning portal that was Indreficus. Hazlik emerged amid the pain-wracked dreamscapes of Indreficus. Unwittingly, Hazlik had entered the Domain of Dread known as the Nightmare Lands, where Indreficus's shrieking and condemnation hounded him. After months of torment, Hazlik escaped into the Mists and eventually emerged into a realm that knew nothing of magic. His Thayan tattoos had turned gold and warped into symbols meaning "traitor. Undaunted, Hazlik used his magic to dominate the people of this new realm, claiming the title of zulkir and dubbing his realm Hazlan HAZLIK's DOMAIN Hazlan is altered by Hazlik’s presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Hazlik can close or open the borders to Hazlan at will. While the borders are closed, the Mists surrounding Hazlan are filled with monstrous magical experiments. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a tum or starts its turn there must succeed on a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw or take 5 (1410) Force damage and have the Restrained condition until the start of its next turn. A creature makes this save only once per turn. Frayed Magic. Whenever a creature (excluding Hazlik and his allies) casts a spell using a level 5+ spell slot, it rolls on the Wild Magic Surge table in the Player's Handbook to create a magical effect. Magic Nightmares. While in his lair, Veneficus, Hazlik can cast Clairvoyance, requiring no spell components and using the same spellcasting ability as his Spellcasting action. When the spell is cast this way, its range is all of Hazlan, Hazlik can see but can't hear through the sensor, and the sensor can appear only on an Eye of Hazlik. If Hazlik dies, these effects end immediately. The effects resume if Hazlik gains a new body (see his Darklord Restoration trait). HAZLIK's LAIR Hazlik’s crimson-towered palace, Veneficus, stands atop the jagged mesa called the Red Rise. The Darklord is attended by a staff of magical creations and his most accomplished apprentices. VENEFICUS FEATURES Veneficus is littered with frivolous magic effects and magic items that no longer keep Hazlik’s attention. The site is depicted on map 2.16 and has. the following features:

Page 138

Halls of Triumph. Multiple audience chambers, display galleries, and pleasure gardens rival one another in decadence, each exhibiting grotesque artwork and trophies of Hazlik’s achievements. House of Solitude. The secluded House of Solitude is magically locked, and Hazlik forbids anyone from entering. At night, muffled screams and flickering lights emanate from within. Red Tower. Hazlik’s personal chambers fill the Red Tower. Magical portals connect the untold numbers of demiplanar chambers that make up the tower. Somewhere within lies Hazlik’s orrery, a multistory magical device that allows the Darklord to see through any Eye of Hazlik in the domain. ADVENTURES IN HAZLAN Tales in Hazlan involve radical magical experiments, supernatural environments, rivalries between mages, and magical surveillance. Use this, section to develop horror adventures in this domain. The Eye of Hazlik watches the city of Sly-Var and the approaching retinue of one of the Darklord's apprentices # Sam Keiser HORROR IN HAZLAN Adventures set in Hazlan often include the following elements. Eyes OF HAZLIK Hazlik spies on his domain's inhabitants using the Eye of Hazlik. This eyelike sigil adorns buildings, clothing, talismans, and creatures across the domain, Locals claim the symbol protects them from dangerous magic and creatures, Hazlik uses the eyes to spy on useful or threatening mages and to reinforce his reputation as an ever-present overlord. Hazlik often sends loyal apprentices or magical creatures to capture those whose behavior witnessed through the eyes offended him. He turns such individuals into fodder for experimentation in Sly-Var or Veneficus. HazLan WILD Macic ‘As a result of unchecked arcane experimentation, magic functions erratically in Hazlan. Whenever a creature in Hazlan expends a spell slot to cast a spell or activates a magic item, it rolls 1410. On a 1, it unleashes a random magical effect. Roll on the Wild Magic Surge table in chapter 3 of the Player's Handbook to determine the effect. Consider describing these results in unsettling ways that don't change their effects, as in these examples: + Summoned creatures use the proscribed stat blocks but are cosmetically warped to resemble undead or aberrant creatures, + Effects that teleport the caster relocate the caster into a misty expanse full of threatening shapes.

Page 139

+ Effects on creatures cause unsettling sensations, such as the feeling of earwigs wriggling beneath one’s skin or one's teeth struggling free. Anyone who openly wears an Eye of Hazlik can choose not to roll on the Wild Magic Surge table in this way once per day. HAZLIK's APPRENTICES Dozens of mages serve as Hazlik's apprentices. Between infrequent, demeaning group lessons with the Darklord, these apprentices pit themselves against one another to impress Hazlik by pushing the boundaries of magical innovation. Those who gain Hazlik’s favor are promised arcane secrets known only to the Darklord—secrets that might not exist. This creates a cadre of Hazlani mages who recklessly seek to impress Hazlik by the following means, among others: + Changing the nature of reality to suit their whims + Creating monuments in Hazlik’s honor + Devising new magic items, spells, or supernatural materials + Forging unique magical servants for Hazlik * Stealing rare magic items + Unleashing dramatic displays of magical might Hazlik’s current favorite apprentices include the masked wizard Yhal the Skygazer, the shadow-cloaked Ruzelo, and the daring genius Eleni. HAZLAN CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Hazlan often involve mages abusing their power to garner Hazlik’s fickle favor. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-3. The characters arrive in Toyalis, where servants of the mage Khrinitov abduct locals with supposed psychic potential. Eleni, an apprentice of Hazlik, believes Khrinitov's experiments are pointless, She hires the characters to rescue the villagers from Khrinitov's tower amid the Orbitoclasts. In doing so, the characters reveal Khrinitov to be an Intellect Devourer, a Brain in a Jar (see chapter 5), or another aberrant being. Levels 4-8. Eleni reveals that she's trying to undermine Hazlik and protect the people of Hazlan. She tells the characters of a magic mineral in the Mound of the Worm that Purple Worms pursue. She encourages the party to collect the mineral and use the purple worms to destroy locations like the Marks. As the characters do so, they face magical guardians and hazards. During their travel, they're exposed to strange magic and might gain Dark Gift feats (see chapter 1) Levels 9-12. Eleni asks the characters to come to Sly-Var to plan a raid on Hazlik’s palace, There, she reveals that she's been exposing them to. dangerous magic from across the domain. She entraps them with a portal that leads to arena-like laboratories connected to Veneficus, where Hazlik pits them against terrors his other apprentices have created. The characters must escape, potentially facing Eleni and Hazlik along the way. EYES OF THE DARKLORD Adventure for Level 10 Characters Situation. Subjects of magical experimentation seek to escape Hazlan, but every time they try, they're spotted through the Eyes of Hazlik, and the Darklord's apprentices hunt them down Hook. Allies hire the characters to help them escape Hazlan by destroying Hazlik's orrery in Veneficus. They offer to pay the party 4,000 GP once they're guided to another domain.

Page 140

Map 2.16: Veneficus Player Version @ Mike Schley & Mike Schley ENCOUNTERS The adventure uses map 2.16 and consists of these encounters: Greeting at Veneficus, The rooms within Veneficus (see map 2.16) and the walls outside all bear at least one Eye of Hazlik. If the characters are spotted, one of Hazlik’s former apprentices (Medium, Chaotic Evil Archmage) appears, This apprentice has been transformed to look and behave like the Darklord, The duplicate belittles the characters and fights to the death. Galleries of Suffering. As the characters explore Veneficus, roll on the following table to determine what they find within, 146 Encounter 1 ‘Another Archmage that behaves like Hazlik (see above), or a similarly disguised Incubus 2 Artwork that releases a Bone Devil if touched 3 Three Mages arguing about who's greatest 4 ‘Three Waxworks (see chapter §) disguised as mages arguing about who's Hazlik’s favorite 5 One Stone Golem sculpted to look like Hazlik 6 Flawed Magic (see below) Flawed Magic. Among mundane decorations, this room contains a random magic item, Roll on the Arcana~Common table in chapter 7 of the Dungeon Master's Guide. The Item functions once, causes a wild magic surge (see "Hazlan Wild Magic’), and then crumbles to dust. Red Tower. Shifting portals connect the passages in the Red Tower. Every time the characters pass through a portal in the tower, roll 1d8. If the number rolled is lower than the number of portals passed through, the characters arrive in the orrery chamber. On an 8, the characters appear at the entrance to Veneficus, Orrery Chamber. This room is dominated by a magical orrery only Hazlik can use, It is defended by a Relentless Juggernaut. The orrery has AC 19, HP 25, and Immunity to Psychic damage. Every time the orrery takes damage, roll on the Wild Magic Surge table in chapter 3 of the Player's Handbook. Negative effects affect the character who damaged the orrery, while beneficial effects affect the juggernaut. Hazlik. After the orrery is destroyed, Hazlik (see earlier in this chapter) appears and offers a magic-using character power and influence as his apprentice. If the character accepts, he commands that character to murder the others. If the character declines, Hazlik mocks the party. The Darklord uses magic to flee if he takes any damage, swearing revenge. Characters who destroy the orrery and escape Veneficus earn a bonus 3,100 XP. INNSMOUTH Domain of the Imprisoned Apocalyptic Darklord: Cthulhu Genre: Cosmic horror Hallmarks: Cosmic horror creatures, invasive alien dreams, surreal locations, warring cults Mist Talismans: Effigy of Great Cthulhu, starmetal rod or amulet with eldritch runes, sahuagin-tooth fishing lure Aligned Tarokka Card: Bishop Opposed Tarokka Card: Donjon

1) # Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow Hidden amid the Domains of Dread lies a hideous realm defiant of reason and smothered by the Mists. From a violent, murky sea rises a crescent of shattered peaks, mangled spires broken like the jaws of some slain thalassic titan. From noxious fumaroles, miasmic pillars lurch from this accursed isle into a sky inconsistently lit by a distant, drowned light. Strange ruins dot this island's rocky coasts, some suggestive of forgotten peoples, others alien in form and purpose. Massive statues of crimsonflecked bloodstone loom over sheer cliffs, each a towering effigy of some profane monstrosity or alien god. One shore bears the wreckage of a few dozen collapsing wooden structures, Little evidence remains of this settlement’s residents, but at its edge lies a toppled sign bearing a word in Common that gives the island its name: Innsmouth Despite its forsaken appearance, the isle of Innsmouth isn't abandoned, Cults vie for control of the island, among them the servants of Great Cthulhu, a harbinger of world-rending aberrance. At the island's hidden heart, Cthulhu lies dreaming, its alien mind reaching across the Domains of Dread and beyond, drawing to it those who might rouse its full might and unleash it from its prison in the Mists. DOMAIN FEATURES Innsmouth is known for the following features: Cosmic Horror Visions. Cthulhu's dreams draw aberrant beings and shape Innsmouth as an amalgamation of cosmic horrors, from disparate ruins on the surface to stranger realms in the depths. Cult Haven. Evil cults devoted to the entities Cthulhu, Dagon, Olhydra, and Zhudun struggle to control the domain, These cults view strangers as useful allies (see "Cults of Innsmouth’ for details). Dreams of Cthulhu, Cthulhu seeks freedom, It manipulates the dreams of those on the island, sharing visions of their escape from the domain resulting from the same rituals that would free it. LOCATIONS IN INNSMOUTH Innsmouth is an inhospitable expanse of alien mountains and ruin-littered coasts. The village of Innsmouth and the mazelike Vaults are described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Innsmouth Locations table. INNSMOUTH The ruined coastal village of Innsmouth is the most obvious shelter from the storms, alien creatures, and strange events on the island. The Cult of the Chthonic Night knows this and realizes its imprisoned master, Great Cthulhu, calls to strangers to aid the cultists in releasing it. This makes the ruined village a shadowy war zone, where cultists and their servants hunt for fresh acolytes and sacrifices.

Page 142

THE VAULTS Beneath the mountains of Inns mouth winds a labyrinth of improbable passages. Dramatic caverns narrow into the corridors of buried palaces that transform into the wooden halls of forgotten farmhouses. Regularly, these underground paths open into vast cavities, many with their own strange environments, weather, and celestial bodies. What lies among the Vaults? Cultists speak of the realms of alien beings, ghoul empires, countrysides ravaged by sapient energies, the dwellings of reclusive witches, and a thin place in the walls of the prison where Great Cthulhu lies trapped. Rather, this ageless terror remains physically bound and can exert only a fraction of its terrible might. Cthulhu has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects. ACTIONS Multiattack. REGIONAL EFFECTS Innsmouth is altered by Cthulhu's presence, creating the following effects: Choking Depths. A creature can hold its breath for only 30 seconds while in the domain. Closing the Borders. While in its domain, Cthulhu can close or open the borders of Innsmouth at will. While the borders are closed, those who enter the Mists surrounding Innsmouth become lost at sea amid inky fog for 344 days before arriving once more at Innsmouth. Something in the Air. Creatures other than Cthulhu and its allies have Disadvantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Frightened condition. If Cthulhu dies, these effects end immediately. CTHULHU'S TALE ‘Spawned beyond the borders of the multiverse in realities where time and life have little worth, Cthulhu is a servant of elder evils and a harbinger of their inevitable ruin. For untold eons, Cthulhu's will influenced receptive minds across the multiverse, reaching out in dreams and using them to open gateways to the Far Realm. Through such portals, Cthulhu visited the Material Plane, corrupted and laid waste to civilizations, and erected city-size monuments to its mighty predecessors. Such distortion and defilement didn't go unnoticed by the Dark Powers. Multiple times the Mists closed around dread Cthulhu, and multiple times the terror evaded them, It was drawn into the Shadowfell when an extinct cabal of mages summoned it onto a sepulchral world of the Material Plane during a once-in-an-era alignment of eclipses. With the magic of the Shadowfell enhanced, the Mists rose and drew in Cthulhu. Raging and grasping, Cthulhu dragged with it remnants of past conquests and violent minds linked to its own. These disparate nightmares became the foundation of its prison: a surreal island hidden amid the Domains of Dread. CTHULHU'S DOMAIN Innsmouth is altered by Cthulhu’s presence, creating the following effects: Choking Depths. A creature can hold its breath for only 30 seconds while in the domain, Closing the Borders. While in its domain, Cthulhu can close or open the borders of Innsmouth at will, While the borders are closed, those who enter the Mists surrounding Innsmouth become lost at sea amid inky fog for 344 days before arriving once more at Innsmouth. Something in the Air, Creatures other than Cthulhu and its allies have Disadvantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Frightened condition. If Cthulhu dies, these effects end immediately. CTHULHU's LAIR ‘Amid surreal expanses within Innsmouth's depths lies the entrance to Cthulhu's Vault, a prison within the Mists where Cthulhu lies trapped. The entrance to Cthulhu’s Vault is a ritual site etched with symbols of power from across time and hundreds of worlds. Those who perform the proper rituals, guided by visions from Cthulhu itself, might free themselves from the domain of Inns mouth, and, in so doing, unleash Cthulhu on the multiverse. CTHULHU'S VAULT FEATURES Cthulhu's Vault is a hidden realm, but the entrance to it lies amid the Vaults beneath the island. The vault is depicted on map 2.18 and has the following features: Noisome Brine. A dark, frothy waterway winds through the Vaults, ultimately emerging from either the Cleft of Dreams or the mouth of the Drowned Colossus. Ritual Site. A partially toppled monument bearing a tentacled visage marks a weak point in Cthulhu's prison. Before it stands an altar etched with bizarre runes, These symbols are of otherworldly design, but each cult of Innsmouth knows the meaning of a few. Only cultists imbued with the might of all four cults can perform the ritual these runes describe and force open Cthulhu’s prison. The Perceptive. A small ship called the Perceptive lies beached here, a remnant of a group of cultists’ past attempt to access Cthulhu's Vault. The bowsprit is broken, ending in a jagged point. The Providence. The shattered remains of a ship called the Providence serves as the shelter of a mysterious seer. It's said this former cultist knows more of Cthulhu's Vault than any other creature. CULTS OF INNSMOUTH Four cults operate in Innsmouth, each with its own vile ends. While these groups are ruinously evil, characters might temporarily ally with one or more to oppose another foe or to escape the domain.

Page 146

CULTS AND CULTISTS The following groups vie for power on and under Innsmouth. Following each cult's name are the ritual words cult members use in their blasphemous practices, along with other details. CULT OF THE CHTHONIC NIGHT Hail, Hail, Cthulhu Awaits Worships: Cthulhu Leader: King-Priest Eeyog-Yah Goal: Free Cthulhu from imprisonment. Lair: The village of Innsmouth and the Vaults Initiates of the Cult of the Chthonic Night open their minds to the whispers of Great Cthulhu and seek to free the imprisoned terror from the Domains of Dread. Predominantly composed of Aberrant Cultists and Kuo-toa that dwell amid waterlogged ruins deep below the Graven Peak, the cult interprets visions from Cthulhu as omens, calls for sacrifice, and signs of their god's coming. King-Priest Eeyog-Yah, an ageless and desiccated Kuo-toa Archpriest, leads the cult. Eeyog-Yah occupies a state of perpetual somnambulism and narrates its visions. The priest's attendants decipher their leader's rambling as directives from their god. Those who join the cult and participate in its induction rituals hear the voice of Cthulhu more clearly than others do (see "Cult Dark Gifts’) Symbol of the Cult of the Chthonic Night Symbol of the Cult of the Drowning Wave Symbol of the Cult of the Heavenly Doom Symbol of the Cult of the Scaly Abyss # CoupleofKooks CULT OF THE DROWNING WAVE Make Clean for the Aeon Wave Worships: Olhydra Leader: Draa Shoom Goal: Sink innsmouth below the waves Lair: Eyes of Olhydra Those who await the Drowning Wave seek to make Innsmouth and all its inhabitants part of Olhydra's realm on the Elemental Plane of Water. Olhydra, the Prince of Evil Water, then could use the might of Cthulhu and the mass sacrifice of the island to dominate the Elemental Planes and flood the Material Plane. Draa Shoom, the cult's Aboleth leader, believes Olhydra will return the cult to a place of dominance over countless worlds in return for its sacrifice. To effect this, Draa Shoom coaxes Elemental Cultists and shipwrecked individuals to the Eyes of Olhydra, There, amid boggy ruins and the lairs of murky elemental creatures, rises the Fathomless Gate. This ancient stone arch, thought to connect to the Elemental Plane of Water, is covered by bars of starmetal, which prevent passage by solid creatures. The cult seeks the proper rituals to open the gate, allowing Olhydra's scouring waters. to flood the domain. Those who participate in the cult's induction rituals are infused with a draught of Olhydra's sapient form (see "Cult Dark Gifts").

Page 147

CULT OF THE HEAVENLY DooM Empty Yourself to Gorge on the Stars Worships: Zhudun Leader: Celesta Gray! Goal: Open gateways to join Zhudun Lair: Blind Observatory and the Map of the Eclipse Among the broken heights of the domain, the obsessed astronomers of the Cult of the Heavenly Doom seek signs in the heavens and omens from Zhudun, the Corpse Star, an Elder Evil that wanders space and knows the secrets of extinction. These cultists hope to climb into the heavens and join Zhudun. The cult's leader, Celesta Grayl, has sought Zhudun for centuries. While documenting her observations, Grayl discovered a path to immortality and became a Vampire Umbral Lord. Death Cultists, along with desperate and deluded souls, have flocked to the ancient stargazer, forming the cult around Grayl and her discoveries despite their leader's protestations and predations. The vampire suffers the cultists only so long as they help her decipher secrets from ruins around the island and retrieve knowledge from the grip of other cults. She believes their discoveries will help her reveal when the stars are right to join Zhudun in the dark places between the stars, Those who join the cult and participate in its induction rituals release a portion of their life to the stars (see "Cult Dark Gifts") CULT OF THE SCALY AByss The Prince Awaits—Become the Way Worships: Dagon Leader: xrgh Goal: Summon Dagon Lair: Dagon's Claws The followers of Dagon, Demon Prince of the Darkened Depths, lurk among and beneath the waves of Innsmouth, Ancient even among inhabitants of the Abyss, Dagon seeks to drag demon lords and their Abyssal realms into its demesne of Shadowsea. Once sated on the flesh of its foes, Dagon aspires to unleash its might across the multiverse in a sanity-shattering wave, Xr'gh, a Sahuagin Baron fanatically devoted to Dagon, seeks to turn all who dwell beneath the waves to the demon lord's worship. Xr'gh seeks enough sacrifices to tum the sea red with blood, an offering great enough to attract Dagon's notice and draw the demon lord to Innsmouth. The cult leader expects Dagon then to feed on Cthulhu and begin its conquest of the Abyss. Ravenous Sahuagin follow Xr'gh, as do Fiend Cultists and murderers desirous of the eerie gold treasures the sahuagin offer from their submerged vaults. The Cult of the Scaly Abyss hunts the domain's ruined villages, abducting anyone who seeks shelter amid the wreckage. Those they find, they drag through secret paths beneath the waves to their ritual site under the island called Dagon's Claw. Those who join the cult and participate in its induction rituals develop shark-like organs (see "Cult Dark Gifts"). Cutr RITES The cults of Innsmouth seek initiates to aid in their unfathomable works. Those who prove themselves reliable devotees gain access to the transformative power of strange gods. Cur RENOWN The cults of Innsmouth reward those who honor their obscene patrons, Use the guidance in the Dungeon Master's Guide to track characters’ individual standing within Innsmouth's cults. Characters can gain renown with a cult by performing deeds such as those in the Innsmouth Cults Renown table. Innsmouth Cults Renown Deed Renown Gained Slaying an enemy cultist* "1 Despoiling an enemy ritual site* +1 Participating in a cult ritual* "1 Ritual sacrifice of an enemy or magic item +2 Retrieving alien devices +2 Disrupting an enemy ritual 43 Slaying the leader of an enemy cult +5 *Renown for this deed can be gained only once per week.

Curr Dark GIFTS Characters who have a Renown Score of 10+ with an Innsmouth cult are honored as the chosen of the cult's patron. They are urged to take part in a ritual that inducts them into the cult and bestows on them a supernatural boon. A character who participates in such a ritual gains the Dark Gift (see chapter 1) noted on the Innsmouth Cults Dark Gifts table. Innsmouth Cults Dark Gifts Cult Dark Gift Cult of Chthonic Night Gathered Whispers Cult of the Drowning Wave Symbiotic Being Cult of the Heavenly Doom Touch of Death Cult of the Scaly Abyss Aberrant Anatomy These Dark Gifts include magical transformations associated with the cult’s patron and its members. This magic can make it easier to navigate parts of the domain and bypass seals made by cult magic. This includes the binding sigils that mark Cthulhu’s Vault. A character with one of these Dark Gifts can't gain another. A character with one of these gifts who leaves Innsmouth can choose to lose the Dark Gift or keep it. ADVENTURES IN INNSMOUTH ‘Adventures in Innsmouth involve cults, degenerate rituals, nightmarish visions, and aberrant transformations. Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain, —_ Nightmarish monuments and reality-defying ruins cover the island called Innsmouth @ David Frasheski CorruPTIVE HoRROR The cults of Innsmouth are monstrous and dangerous, but not unwelcoming. Each seeks to bring a different terrifying cataclysm to Innsmouth, As characters interact with these cults and learn more of the island's secrets, bizarre dreams encourage the characters to accept the Dark Gifts the cults offer and balance rivalries to gain the might of each, Groups that engage with each cult might have strange adventures across Inns mouth and undergo bizarre transformations. Those that do will find themselves with the power to free Cthulhu from the island~and maybe themselves as well DREAMS OF INNSMOUTH Cthulhu sends dreams to those in Innsmouth—and in realms beyond—that might help it escape its prison. These dreams include strange images spurring the dreamers to seek the cults of Innsmouth, gain their powers, and then dispel the sigils in Cthulhu’s Vault, Dreams sent by Cthulhu might focus on the following topics and imagery:

Page 149

Cult of the Chthonic Night: The village of Innsmouth, the Graven Peak, desiccated fish, tentacles Cult of the Drowning Wave: The Eyes of Olhydra, shipwrecks, tidal waves, three eyes underwater Cult of the Heavenly Doom: The peaks of Innsmouth, corpses in space, monoliths, whirling stars Cult of the Scaly Abyss: Dagon's Claws, deep-sea predators, sharks eating sharks, undersea ruins Cthulhu's Vault: The Cleft of Dreams, the Drowned Colossus, dark passages, strange sigils Any of these dreams might end with a vision of the Mists parting and the characters’ home or a similarly tantalizing location appearing beyond. OLD One's BARGAIN Cthulhu’s dream-messages tempt characters to release the entity to win their own escape from its domain. Whether Cthulhu can or will fulfil this offer is up to you, but the characters might not wish to jeopardize reality to escape. In the course of exploring Innsmouth, they can find the writings of obsessed wizards and cult heresies that suggest the following secrets: Cthulhu’s Disruption. Cthulhu’s power is such that, should someone release it, the Mists around Innsmouth would open and those in the domain could escape Cthulhu’s Might. Cthulhu wouldn't emerge from its prison at full strength. For approximately one hour after its release, heroes might force Cthulhu back into its prison, should they subject the terror to potent magic such as Imprisonment or Wish, or a blow powerful enough to disrupt its physical form, like a powerful lightning strike, a massive explosion, or a strike from a large sailing ship. Elaborate on these possibilities as characters explore Innsmouth, and encourage them to believe they might be able to outwit a Great Old One, OTHERWORLDLY RELICS The monsters noted in the "Cosmic Horror" section of chapter 4 make appropriate inhabitants of the ruins and vaults of Innsmouth. These beings often possess evidence of alien technologies and cultures. Innsmouth's cults covet such relics, Roll on or choose a result from the Alien Relics table to inspire what otherworldly beings might possess. Alien Relics 146 The Alien Being Possesses... 1 A crystalline wand with a monstrous face. 2 A faintly glowing trapezohedron. 3 Ajade amulet shaped like a sphinx. 4 ‘A metallic cluster of globes and half globes. 5 ‘An oversize, pattern-etched silver key, 6 A preserved brain in a glass cylinder. INNSMOUTH CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Innsmouth involve warring cults, unfathomable beings, and bizarre transformations. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-2. The characters wash up on Innsmouth island and shelter in the village. They experience strange dreams. Soon after, they encounter cultists who seek to initiate them and others who seek to sacrifice them. The characters gain allies among one of the island's cults. Levels 3-5. The allied cult sends the characters to disrupt other cults and retrieve relics from the ruins. In return, the cult offers insights into the characters’ dreams and suggests ways to escape the island, After proving their worth, a character receives the cult’s Dark Gift (see "Cult Rites"). Levels 6-16. Led by their dreams and duplicitous cult members, the characters move from cult to cult, gaining influence by delving into ruins and strange vaults. In doing so, they collect Dark Gifts, as well as hints about Cthulhu's Vault and ways to undermine the Great Old One (see "Old One's Bargain’). Levels 17-20. After collecting a Dark Gift from each cult, the characters follow a treacherous path through the Vaults to Cthulhu’s Vault. There, they can risk releasing Cthulhu to escape the island. VAULT OF THE ELDER Adventure for Level 11 Characters Situation. Cult lore tells of a vault that is home to a wise elder who knows the secrets of escaping the island. Hook. A cult leader shares directions to the vault but wants one of the strange relics the elder is said to hoard.

KALAKERI Domain of Betrayal and Revenge Darklord: Ramya Vasavadan Adventure Genres: Gothic horror and dark fantasy Hallmarks: Monstrous leaders, family intrigue, war-torn nation Mist Talismans: Rusted tulwar, shield with a wyvern-and-lotus emblem, a polished skull Aligned Tarokka Card: Avenger Opposed Tarokka Card: Artifact sesApueniger # Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow Deceptively beautiful, Kalakeri is a land of verdant rainforests and a web of rivers and lakes known as the Backwaters. At the height of its prosperity, Kalakeri was a robust center of art, commerce, and religion, with merchants spreading wild tales of the land, using fanciful names such as Sri Raji and the Steaming Lands. Now Kalakeri is a conflict-torn shadow of its past glory. The remnants of the Vasavadan dynasty—three heirs transformed into monsters by their depravity and hatred-trap the land in an endless cycle of war, The people of Kalakeri live in fear of Maharani Ramya Vasavadan, the domain's Darklord, as well as her treacherous siblings, Arijani and Reva. The Darklord's curse manifests both in her deathless desire to rule Kalakeri and in her siblings’ endless rebellions. Both sides escalate their atrocities against one another, drawing the powerful and the innocent alike into their squabbles. As the maharanis siblings court allies, the Darklord's forces hunt and execute renegades, adding skulls to the grim Tower of Traitors, DOMAIN FEATURES Kalakeri is known for the following features: Factions in Conflict. Two factions claim Kalakeri's Sapphire Throne: the soldiers and loyalists of Maharani Ramya Vasavadan, and the nobles and merchants who support the younger royal siblings, Arijani and Reva Vasavadan. Jadurai, a City at War, The vast city of Jadurai is Kalakeri’s capital, the site of the royal palace, and a battlefield where factions struggle to claim or hold it as a symbol of rule. ‘The Backwaters. Rare creatures and extraordinary magical plants flourish in the Backwaters, the collective name for the wildemess away from Kalakeris cities. Scattered refugee groups hide in the wilds and organize dangerous, desperate attacks on the Vasavadan factions.

Page 152

LOCATIONS IN KALAKERI Tropical rainforests isolate Kalakeri's communities. While the domain's residents are adept at navigating these wildernesses, strangers often fall prey to the domain's myriad hazards and predators. The capital of Jadurai is detailed below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Kalakeri Locations table. JADURAI Ancient structures adorned with the artistry of past dynasties stand alongside modem buildings in Kalakeri’s capital, Jadurai. The city retains its splendor, but the conflict between the Vasavadan siblings takes an enormous toll as they struggle over the Cerulean Citadel, the nation's capitol, as well as the Sapphire Throne within, Sections of the city lie in ruins. On the outskirts of Jadurai rises the Tower of Traitors, Rows of skulls taken from the Darklord's enemies fill depressions in this stone tower, Two skulls hold places of special prominence: the original human skulls of Arijani and Reeva Vasavadan. The Darklord's skeletal soldiers build the ‘tower as part of her plan to rid Kalakeri of evil and receive the gods' blessings. Ramya'’s TALE The Vasavadan dynasty has ruled Kalakeri since ancient times. Ramya Vasavadan was handpicked by her father to succeed him, but on the bleak day of his death, Ramya's brother, Arijani, declared himself the new maharaja Ramya refused to yield to her brother, and with her allies, she captured and prepared to execute him. But Ramya's sister, Reeva, counseled mercy. Instead, Ramya imprisoned Arijani and ascended as Kalakeri’s maharani, Queen of Kings. Inthe years that followed, Ramya ruled justly. But in the shadows, Reeva plotted with rebels, who eventually freed Arijani and spurred nobles across the nation to revolt. Regretting her mercy, Ramya sent her army to crush the rebels and mercilessly execute the treacherous. Reeva and Arijani combated Ramya’s superior forces with ambushes and deceptions. Once again exploiting Ramya's sympathies, the pair tricked Ramya, invaded the Cerulean Citadel, and executed their sister by strangling. With her final gasps, Ramya cursed her brother and sister, calling them bloodthirsty beasts, Soon after, a terrible monsoon struck Kalakeri, bringing with it the Mists. The storm lashed Kalakeri for weeks. At its height, Ramya returned from the dead and called on those who had fallen in her service. Loyal in death, a corpse legion joined the revitalized maharani, besieged the Cerulean Citadel, and slaughtered Arijani and Reva. Ramya then stole their skulls and built them into the foundations of what would become the Tower of Traitors. Arijani and Reeva’s plots didn't end with their lives, They too were reborn, transformed into monstrous reflections of their ravenous desires. Now in the Land of the Mists, the conflict gripping Kalakeri continues, trapping the nation in an endless war and forcing its people to swear loyalty to monsters. Ramya's DoMAIN While Ramya is in control of the Sapphire Throne, Kalaker' is altered by Ramya's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in her domain, Ramya can close or open the borders to Kalakeri at will. While the borders are closed, the Mists surrounding Kalakeri sap the life of those within them. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there and isn't a Construct or an Undead must succeed on a DC 20 Constitution saving throw or gain 1 Exhaustion level. A creature makes this save only once per turn, Everlasting Army. A Medium or Small Humanoid slain in combat rises 24 hours later as a Wight under Ramya's control, unless the Humanoid is restored to life or its body is destroyed If Ramya dies or if her siblings control the Sapphire Throne, these effects end immediately. Ramya’s LAIR The Cerulean Citadel is Kalakeri's royal palace. It derives its name from the blue sandstone used in its construction, Control of the citadel and the Sapphire Throne within regularly passes between Ramya and her siblings. Bas-reliefs adorn the palace, depicting the history, heroes, and legends of the kingdom. The site is depicted on map 2.20 and has the following features: Domed Court. The central domed court houses the Sapphire Throne, It hosts guardians and treasures gathered by the paranoid Vasavadans. Towers guard the surrounding walls, which are accessible via bridges from the court's upper floors, War-Torn Courtyards. Within the palace walls lie marble pavilions arranged among baths, ponds, and gardens. Some are intact, but sieges and repeated conflicts have ravaged others. ADVENTURES IN KALAKERI Adventures set in Kalakeri involve suspicion and power struggles. Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain.

Page 156

vidence of flict between the Vasavadan siblings lies rotting before the Cerulean Citade| # Miro Petrov HORROR IN KALAKERI Adventures in Kalakeri often include the following elements. ARIJANI AND REEVA Ramya's rule is jeopardized by her siblings’ pursuit of power. Arijani and Reeva Vasavadan promise their followers wealth and freedom, but in truth, their agendas serve only them. Ramya still loves her siblings, making her vulnerable to their promises and lies. So long as Ramya exists, the Dark Powers resurrect Arjjani and Reeva as if they had Ramya's Darklord Restoration trait. Arijani, Arijani is the charming face of resistance for all who oppose Ramya. Now a Rakshasa, gregarious Arljani has decadent tastes. When in control of the Cerulean Citadel, he hosts parties for Kalakeri's elite that conceal private feasts featuring obscene delicacies and mortal flesh, Reeva, Reeva's adoration of Ramya gave way to jealousy when Ramya eclipsed her in thelr family’s esteem. Now an Arcanaloth, Reeva uses insidious magic from beneath the Cerulean Citadel to summon fiends and manipulate foes from afar. Behind her back, many mockingly call Reva “Inajira’ her charismatic Arijani's opposite. RENOWN IN KALAKERI You can track the characters’ influence with Ramya's loyalist faction and her siblings’ rebel faction, using the renown rules from the Dungeon Master's Guide to track the characters’ standing with these groups. Characters might gain renown with either faction by performing missions such as these: + Capturing or killing a rival agent Planting evidence that a rival committed a crime Conscripting a powerful ally for the faction Revealing the location of a rival's hold Revealing traitors within the faction Anytime the characters complete a mission for either group, their Renown Score with that group increases by 1, but their Renown Score with the other group decreases by 1 (to a minimum of 0). After the first time characters gain renown with either faction, any faction the characters have a Renown Score of 0 with treat the characters as follows: Loyalist Faction. Members of this faction are Hostile toward the characters, The faction sends troops of Wights or other Undead to capture the characters weekly.

Page 157

Rebel Faction. Members of this faction are Hostile toward the characters, The faction sends Assassins or Wraiths under Reeva's command to kill the characters weekly. Characters can gain renown with a faction they have a Renown Score of 0 with by presenting proof of an action that undermines the other group. Even if betrayed, the factions seek the party's aid, as they view the characters as useful pawns. If the characters have a Renown Score of 5+ with either group, their deeds might benefit the faction enough for it to take control of Jadurai and claim the Sapphire Throne. The longer either faction controls the capital, the more extreme its reign becomes, its atrocities escalating week after week. The characters must balance aiding and opposing both factions to maintain equilibrium, KALAKERI CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Kalakeri typically involve balancing the horrors committed by the Vasavadan factions. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-4. Agents from the rebel faction contact the characters, presenting Arijani and Reeva Vasavadan as misunderstood heroes seeking to overthrow a tyrant. They entreat the characters to save the heir of an allied noble family from a compound controlled by Ramya's Wight soldiers and return him to a hidden safe house. Levels 5~8. The characters meet Arijani and Reeva in their disguises as charming human royals, They thank the characters for their aid and ask them to reclaim the shield of an ancient hero petrified in the Forest of the Still, hoping the shield will be a symbol of their right to rule, As the characters travel to the forest, a messenger delivers a request from Ramya to do the same. The characters must face Basilisks and the Wraiths of the petrified dead to recover the shield and then decide who to return it to. Levels 9-12. Arijani and Reeva plot to attack Jadurai to claim the Sapphire Throne and request the characters’ help. Ramya’s spies have informed her of this plot, and she also seeks the characters’ aid. In the ensuing conflict, the characters might strike at any of the Vasavadan siblings. TOWER OF TRAITORS Adventure for Level 11 Characters Situation, Arijani and Reeva Vasavadan seek to recover the skulls of their mortal bodies from the Tower of Traitors. They believe the skulls will grant them greater power. Hook. Arijani or Reeva order the characters to recover two skulls from the Tower of Traitors but dont say who the skulls belong to. They offer to pay the party with 10,000 GP in gemstones and a Candle of Invocation, Map 2.20: Tower of Traitors ENCOUNTERS This adventure assumes you are tracking the characters’ renown with Kalaker's loyalist and rebel factions (see "Renown in Kalaker' earlier in this, section). It includes these encounters. ‘Stolen Skulls, The skulls Arijani and Reeva seek are from their own corpses, which were defiled by Ramya. The skulls are among hundreds in the Tower of Traitors. The pair don't reveal who the skulls belong to but insist they're vital to the rebel factior's allies. To aid in the search, the siblings give the characters two Spell Scrolls of Locate Object, However, they don't provide the characters with enough details for the spell to pinpoint the skulls. Learning About the Skuils. The characters must learn three details about the skulls for them to be familiar enough to the characters that a spell like Locate Object can locate them. Characters can learn details in three ways:

Page 158

Research. Characters who spend a day searching for rumors or investigating records in one of Kalakeri’s settlements learn a detail by succeeding on a DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion) check or intelligence (Investigation) check respectively. Renown, A character can spend a day questioning non-Hostile members of a faction. After doing so, the characters learn a detail, but their Renown Score with that faction is reduced by 1. The group can do this only once. Magic. The Legend Lore spell reveals one detail; each character can use this spell once. Each time the party learns a detail about the skulls, reveal a portion of the story from the "Ramya’s Tale" section, obscuring the names of the maharani and the traitors until the end. Tower of Traitors. The Tower of Traitors is depicted on map 2.20 and is guarded by patrols of two Revenants and four Wights. If the characters have a Renown Score of 3+ with the loyalist faction, the undead are Indifferent and tell the party to move along-they fight only if characters linger for more than 5 minutes or remove a skull from the tower. if the characters have less renown with the loyalists, the undead are Hostile. A new patrol appears 2d6 rounds after a patrol is defeated. Searching the Tower. The tower is composed of thousands of skulls, and the ones the rebels want can be found via magic such as the Locate Object spell. Once discovered, a skull can be removed in 1 minute using a sharp implement. When both skulls are freed, the hatred lurking in the skulls manifests as two Hostile Gallows Speakers (see chapter 5). Loyalty or Treachery. The next time the characters rest, Arljani (Rakshasa) appears and demands the skulls, If the characters give him the skulls, he compensates them and the characters’ Renown Score with the rebel faction increases by 4, If the characters refuse to give him the skulls, Arijani fights them. The party's Renown Score with the rebels decreases by 4 if Arijani is slain, Soon after, Ramya invites the characters to discuss future plans, Regardless of how the adventure concludes, each character earns a bonus 4,100 XP. KARTAKASS Domain of Tarnished Dreams Darklord: Harkon Lukas Adventure Genres: Dark fantasy and gothic horror Hallmarks: Hidden identities, dangerous performances, exploitative ambitions, werewolves Mist Talismans: Handbill advertising remarkable performances, rustic woodcut of hunting wolves, wolf's tooth necklace Aligned Tarokka Card: Swashbuckler Opposed Tarokka Card: Beast # Pam Wishbow @ Pam Wishbow Life in Kartakass feels staged and surreal, as every plant, beast, and person strives to prove their greatness. Trees and flowers erupt into bloom; songbirds sing themselves hoarse; and every local knows that dreams, fame, and immortal adulation are theirs should they prove worthy. And where talent and expertise fail, obsession and duplicity reign, leading to cycles of triumph, betrayal, and despair. Predators flourish in this land of unrealistic hopes, consuming passions, and vicious secrets.

Page 159

With each full moon, the truth of Kartakass is exposed, as the domain's many werewolves and other lycanthropes indulge their hunger for dominance and blood. After the full moon passes, the idyllic facade returns. Locals don't speak of why most bar their doors and shutter their windows on the nights of the full moon, but they acknowledge that the path to greatness is messy. DOMAIN FEATURES Kartakass is known for the following features: False Idylis. Kartakass is supemnaturally picturesque and outwardly serene. Every season is strikingly beautiful, communities appear idyllic, and many animals are affected as if by the Awaken spell. This makes deceptions and violence all the more stark. Meistersingers. Kartakass has no unified government. Each settlement is governed by a meistersinger, the acknowledged mayor and embodiment of local art. Meistersingers maintain creative rivalries with one another largely for show. ‘Theatricality, Settlements pride themselves on performative traditions, such as distinctive dress, traditional dances, or song stylings, Wolves and Werewolves. Wolves roam the land freely, and Werewolves lurk in the domain's settlements. Residents seldom venture outside during the full moon, fearing the lycanthropes’ hunger. LOCATIONS IN KARTAKASS Kartakass Is a land of rolling hills, light forests, clear lakes, and pastoral farmlands. Communities are welcoming and host frequent festivals. The settlements of Harmonia and Skald are detailed below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Kartakass Locations table. HARMONIA Artists vie to perform at the town of Harmonia's popular Crystal Club, a members-only venue inside a cave that features a stage constructed from a massive geode, The club features dramatic decor and discerning clientele, Rumors say that performers who fail to impress are exiled, The bloodier truth is that the club restricts membership to lycanthropes. Talented artists gain the curse and join their ranks, while the mediocre get devoured. SKALD The largest community in Kartakass, Skald is a bustling hub of commerce and creativity. The lure of overnight celebrity and fawning crowds attracts the ambitious. Yet behind the facade of creativity and freethinking, business owners and celebrities prey on creativity, while performers struggle viciously over paltry opportunities. Vice, criminality, dark bargains, and supernatural predators flourish amid Skald's many stages and frequent festivals. Among Skald's most popular venues is the Old Kartakan Inn and Taverna, owned by the performer and Darklord Harkon Lukas. lnc ri @ Marco Bernardini # Marco Bernardini Kartakass Locations Location Description Argent Falls Performers flock to a bardic college atop the Argent Falls, Caverns behind the falls bear rich silver deposits but are Conservatory haunted by Banshees—the souls of failed performers. HARKON LUKAS Darklord Who Preys on Talent f Igor krstic “I must say, your skill is unlike any I've ever seen, The name's Harkon. I'm sure you've heard of me." — Harkon Lukas

Page 161

The charming werewolf Harkon Lukas manipulates those around him to satisfy his narcissistic needs for adulation and control. Endlessly seeking fame, Harkon travels widely, earning applause and accolades. Yet, whenever he returns to a town he's performed in, he finds he's been forgotten. Lukas believes he needs just the right song or inspiration to make himself unforgettable. To that end, he searches for talented protégés, cultivating their skills, then stealing their work. He ensures their loyalty by gifting them copies of his cursed wolf-tooth necklace, Harkon's Bite, or by transforming them into werewolves. He then manipulates these victims into believing only he will accept them Harkon wears a signature wide-brimmed hat and wolf-tooth necklace, and he carries a violin he calls Bleeding Heart. He uses flattery to charm those he takes an interest in. A skilled artist who declines the offer to become his protégé often earns his obsession. The Darklord might go to any extreme to claim such performers’ talents as his own.

REGIONAL EFFECTS Kartakass is altered by Harkon's presence, creating the following effects: Beast Spies. From dawn until dusk, Tiny Beasts in the domain magically gain the ability to understand Common and can communicate telepathically with other Beasts and Monstrosities. Children of the Night. Harkon exerts influence over the animals of his domain. From dusk until dawn, Medium or smaller Beasts within 1 miles of Harkon have the Charmed condition. If Harkon pleases, these animals become Hostile to creatures other than Harkon and his allies. Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Harkon can close or open the borders to Kartakass at will. While the borders are closed, a strange melody fills the Mists surrounding Kartakass. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn in them must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw, or it has the Unconscious condition for 1d6 hours, then awakes within sight of the town of Harmonia in Kartakass, A creature makes this save only once per turn If Harkon dies, these effects end immediately. HARKON'S TALE Harkon Lukas weaves numerous self-aggrandizing tales of his origins, Among them, Harkon tells of being spurned for his ambitions by the werewolves that raised him. Through talent and sacrifice, he became a legendary performer, and people flocked to bask in his remarkable presence. Eventually, the rulers of Harkon's homeland realized the threat posed by Harkon's growing fame. Officials tried to imprison him for invented crimes, but Harkon's fans defended him. When royal agents came for him, Harkon feigned his death, and his followers revolted. When outraged masses bearing Harkor's coffin besieged the royal palace, Harkon ended his ploy, He burst from his crimson coffin in wolf form and devoured the land's monarch. Drenched in blood, the “resurrected” Harkon returned to his human form and donned the monarch's crown amid the cheers of his deluded followers. Yet before Harkon's reign could begin, the Mists rose around him. When they cleared, Harkon found himself in an unfamiliar land called Kartakass, HarKON's DOMAIN Kartakass is altered by Harkon's presence, creating the following effects: Beast Spies. OLD KARTAKAN INN AND TAVERNA Welcoming and rustic, the Old Kartakan features subtle wolf-themed decor. The site is depicted on map 2.22 and has the following features: Bedrooms. Among the drinks the taverna offers is Meekulbrau, a blood-red berry wine that soothes the throat and improves vocal performances. Cynthia Sheppard “Explain why you have interrupted my reverie, or | will turn your waking hours into nightmares." — Lord Soth Lord Loren Soth seethes within a realm that mocks and frustrates him, Having known the rigid standards of knighthood and warfare, Soth finds Sithicus empty and chaotic, his people apathetic and undisciplined. The elven beauty that once captivated him lies ruined, the land burns with the flames of Krynn's Cataclysm, and the realm's shadows remind Soth of his past failures. Worse, no worthy foes exist to challenge him. So Soth broods on his throne in Nedragaard Keep, haunted by banshees that sing the tale of his disgrace and by rotted, undead copies of the knights who once served him, Lord Soth cares nothing for his domain. But should noteworthy threats or remarkable individuals appear, he seeks to crush or enlist them. The only exception is the spirit of his treacherous former ally, Caradoc. Soth’s undead servants and his werebadger seneschal, Azrael Dax, scour the land for the elusive ghost. Soth shares his imprisonment in Sithicus with all in the domain by keeping its borders perpetually closed. Only those who rouse him to activity or invade Nedragaard Keep might convince him to let them escape. This risks provoking the mighty death knight's wrath, but Soth still thinks of himself as a true knight—he might assist those who present themselves as honorable warriors or show knowledge of his home (see "Influencing Lord Soth’)

‘VILLAIN OF Two WorLps Lord Soth features in adventures in both the Dragonlance and Ravenloft campaign settings. If you want to give Soth a unique relationship to the Domains of Dread, consider these possibilities: + The nature of Soth's curse creates aspects of the death knight in both Krynn and Sithicus. When Soth broods in his fortress in Krynn, he remains active in Sithicus. When the evil god, Takhisis, calls for his service on Krynn, Soth enters a magical torpor in his fortress in Sithicus. + Soth traveled from Krynn to Sithicus and is destined to return. If he leaves Sithicus, the domain might be destroyed, or a villain like Inza Kulchevich might take over. + Soth was trapped in Sithicus, escaped, and was trapped again, perpetuating a cycle of despair. Soths story and where characters encounter him are for you to detail in your adventures. Lorb Sotu's TALE Lord Soth was given a quest to save the people of his world, Krynn, from the wrath of the gods. Possessed by baseless paranoia and possessiveness of his elf wife, Soth forsook that quest, and the world was devastated in a great Cataclysm. In punishment, Soth was resurrected as a death knight and, for centuries, brooded on his disgrace in the charred ruins of his fortress, Dargaard Keep. When the evil god Takhisis whispered to Soth, promising to restore his glory and avenge him against the heroic knights who banished him long ago, Soth answered and served Takhisis. During this time, he called on his undead servants, including the ghost of the knight Caradoc. Seeking to forge a new knighthood of loyal dead, Soth became obsessed with Dragon Highlord Kitiara Uth Matar and charged Caradoc with claiming her soul When Kitiara died in battle, Caradoc refused to capture her spirit unless Soth bargained with Takhisis to restore Caradoc to life. Enraged, Soth tried to destroy Caradoc as the Mists rose around them When the Mists faded, Soth found himself in the domain of Barovia. For a time, he found an ally in Count Strahd von Zarovich, but the two villains couldn't abide one another for long. Soth eventually pursued rumors of his treacherous servant, Caradoc, into the Mists. When the Mists parted again, the domain of Sithicus spread before him, LorbD SoTH's DoMAIN Sithicus is altered by Lord Soth's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in his domain, Lord Soth can close or open the borders to Sithicus. While the borders are closed, purple flames fill the Mists surrounding Sithicus. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 5 (110) Fire damage plus 5 (110) Necrotic damage. Fearless Soldiers. Undead allies of Lord Soth have Immunity to the Frightened condition. Guilt of Sithicus. Few sleep soundly in Sithicus. Lorp Sotu's LAIR Nedragaard Keep Tits Lunter Lord Soth rules from Nedragaard Keep, a twisted, copy of his accursed fortress on Krynn, Dargaard Keep. Nedragaard Keep grows and withers over time like a living thing. Undead soldiers patrolling the keep regularly forget their duties or collapse into lifelessness. The disorder within the rigid death knight's fortress endlessly frustrates Lord Soth, NEDRAGAARD KEEP Nedragaard Keep is a towerlike fortress of crumbling, flame-scarred stone, Prominent locations in the keep are depicted on map 2.31 and have the following features: Gatehouse, Undead soldiers guard the gates to Nedragaard Keep, demanding to know trespassers’ identities. Those who identify themselves with the proper passphrase, such as ‘a seeker of the rose" or “a servant of Nightlund's lord,’ are admitted. Stairs beyond the gate climb several floors to the top of the keep's walls. Garden. Gardens on the keep’s second floor are overgrown with thorny plants—use the rules for razorvine from the Dungeon Master's Guide. From here spread massive vines that bloom with black roses and strangle the entire castle. Throne Room. High in the keep is Soth's throne room. Here Lord Soth spends much of his time dwelling on failures and twisted fantasies. Thirteen Banshees haunt the keep, and at any time, at least three linger here, singing of Soth’s disgrace. ADVENTURES IN SITHICUS

Page 168

‘Adventures set in Sithicus feature mysterious shadows, grim ruins, and strange foes from the warped memories of Lord Soth, Use this section to. develop horror adventures in this domain. Horror IN SITHICUS Adventures in Sithicus often include the following elements. CATACLYSMIC FLAMES Illusory violet flames burn across Sithicus—fires from the Cataclysm that devastated Krynn, Powerful shadows guard prominent, bonfire-like pillars of these flames. These shadows might use the stat blocks of any monster, but they resemble dragons, knights, and figures from Soth's past. Whenever someone defeats one of these guardians, the flame it protected reveals an illusory scene from the Darklord's past before vanishing. Any character who witnesses such a scene learns something of Soths history, the world he comes from, or the knighthood he once belonged to. One of the viewers then gains the Charm of the Black Rose (see "Supernatural Gifts" in the Dungeon Master's Guide for additional details). INFLUENCING LorD SOTH Lord Soth is typically Hostile to strangers, but characters can adjust his attitude to Indifferent by succeeding on a DC 20 Charisma (Deception or Persuasion) check. Soth might employ characters he deems worthy on quests, such as the hunt for the ghost Caradoc. Making a second successful DC 20 Charisma (Deception or Persuasion) check while offering Soth something he covets can improve Soth's attitude to Friendly for a short time, during which he might let others leave his domain. Livinc SHADOWS Shadows in Sithicus often act with a life of their own. Roll on or choose a result from the Shadows of Sithicus table to inspire these shapes’ behavior, ‘Shadows of Sithicus 1d6 The Shadows Behave As... 1 An elf desperately offering a swaddled bundle, 2 Glimpses of skeletal warriors or horses. FT Knights in battle or in the midst of a siege. 4 Peasants being consumed by shadowy flames. 5 Marching soldiers with dragon-like features. 6 Solemn elf priests being ambushed by ogres. SrTHICUS CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Sithicus often involve trying to gain Lord Soth’s favor and win escape from the domain. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-4, Azrael Dax, Lord Soth’s werebadger seneschal, brusquely recruits the characters to hunt down deadly shadows. These shadows take ‘the form of and behave as noble knights. Level 5-9. Impressed by the characters, Azrael sends them to Inza Kulchevich to ask the witch to use her powers to find Lord Soth's elusive former servant, Caradoc. Inza can do this, but first she requires the characters to collect the cataclysmic flame from three ruins across the domain in a magical iron pendant. Each flame is guarded by a shadow creature that resembles some foe from Soth’s twisted memories—like a lance-wielding Warrior Commander or a pompous Archpriest. Levels 10-14, Inza uses the pendant to reveal that Caradoc's sou! is trapped in a black sapphire that Azrael already possesses. The characters must recover the gem. They might then infiltrate Nedragaard Keep to offer it to Lord Soth. Soth is fickle though, and this approach might end in a confrontation with the death knight, NEDRAGAARD KEEP NEDRAGAARD KEEP. rrr ie Map 2.31: Nedragaard Keep Player Version J NerdyMaps Af NerdyMeps

TEPEST Domain of Nature's Cruel Secrets

Page 170

Darklord: Mother Lorinda Adventure Genre: Folk horror Hallmarks: Fey bargains, rural cults, secret sacrifices, sinister festivals Mist Talismans: Bloodstained farm implement, dried crown of white camellias, straw doll Aligned Tarokka Card: Druid Opposed Tarokka Card: Executioner a ‘ we xecutionee ®) # Pam Wishbow (# Pam Wishbow Alls well in Tepest, Fields shimmer with bountiful harvests, and horned village children recite sing-song rhymes. But those who linger among this, land's pastures and colorful cottages can't escape the feeling of being watched or the impression that strange forms move amid the fields. Locals dismiss such worries as the work of fey tricksters, but their smiles fall to mask the desperation in their eyes. Brutality wears a welcoming face in Tepest, a truth embodied by the ancient hag Lorinda, who betrayed her coven in pursuit of adoration. Taking the guise of a deity called Mother, Lorinda adopted the village of Viktal, protecting its people from nature's whims so they can worship her even as they sacrifice themselves to feed her monstrous offspring. In the wilderness beyond, resentful fey watch and plot, offering cruel bargains to those who stray beyond Mother's sight, The people of Viktal do what they must to survive, using tradition to cloak their fear and complicity in a cycle of murder. Strangers become symbols of hope for Viktal'scitizens—welcome alternatives to slaughtering their families in Mother's name. DOMAIN FEATURES Tepest is known for the following features: Children of the Fey. Few children are born in Viktal, Would-be parents must bargain with Mother or the fey to bring them a child. Fey Threats. Caves dot the land, connecting to depths where dangerous fey dwell. The people of Viktal fear them and blame them for every ill. The Last Village, Viktal is the only community in Tepest. The others displeased Mother and suffered terrible fates, Mother. Viktal’s residents devoutly follow the optimistic faith of the nature deity called Mother. Mother's Minders. Mother's most zealous faithful are called Mother's Minders. These devotees sacrifice their left eyes to Mother to show their faith, The Tithe. Viktal's festivals include a fertility celebration called the Tithe that occurs once a season. LOCATIONS IN TEPEST The valley of Tepest is rocky, ill-suited to farming, and plagued by dangerous fey. Nevertheless, those who worship Mother are protected from the worst of the land's dangers. Mother's Sight and Viktal are detailed below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Tepest Locations table.

Page 171

Motuer's SIGHT A mile outside Viktal, in a field apart from the surrounding farms, stands Mother's Sight: an array of rune-etched standing stones. These stones rise as tall as thirty feet, and several have pieces that defy gravity. Within the central and largest stone floats a disk reminiscent of a great eye. Here the people come to worship, celebrate, and sacrifice. Colorful crafts adorn long tables, where the villagers play games and feast in celebration of Mother's bounty, Meals here are tasty, and guests are encouraged to eat and drink beyond their fill. During rituals here, Viktal's community leaders give thanks to Mother before a dramatic fire surrounded by an eight-foot scaffold, (See "The Tithe" adventure for additional details.) VIKTAL Mother ensures peace in Viktal. The village consists of a few dozen cottages, a market square, and an inn on the banks of Lake Kronov. Wicker scarecrows guard the rich fields blessed by Mother, and friendly people go about their daily lives. Cheery villagers welcome visitors, show them hospitality, and demonstrate interest in their stories. Page 172

Brian Valenzuela “What precocious moppets you are! Little ones who show Mother love get rewarded, but bad children get punished. Which sorts of tots are you?" — Mother Lorinda Mother Lorinda desires a family over all else, not realizing she’s destroyed every family she's ever had. A creator of monstrosities, this ancient green hag obsesses over unnatural maternalistic pursuits. Over generations, Tepest's villagers have come to view Lorinda not as a powerful witch, but as a god of the land and fertility called Mother. Lorinda doesn't dissuade her fragile mortal followers from their baseless cult practices. The villagers provide willing sacrifices for the hag's flawed magical attempts to create life, and their adoration gradually soothes the violent, magical outbursts that follow her failures Lorinda’s walking tower of bones, the Gurgyl, ranges across Tepest. Those who seek her here find a distracted but deceptively cunning hag clinging to grotesque dolls of her family. She laments the loss of her sisters, Laveeda and Leticia, never acknowledging that they remain alive as her prisoners. She often speaks of her daughter, Laoirse (pronounced "LUR-shuh’)—the name she uses over and over again for each of her monstrous creations. When Lorinda visits Viktal, she appears in her illusory guise as Mother, an ancient, powerful woman wearing a mothlike cloak and bearing a branch with burning limbs. She calls on the people to prove their love for her through sacrifice, whether of food and crafts, their left eyes, or their lives Sometimes her arrival is sudden and terrifying, such as when she magically appears to crawl from one of her followers’ empty eye sockets. Lorinda seeks new ways to create a permanent family. This might involve great sacrifices, stolen fey magic, strange adoptions, or all manner of supernatural bargains.
