REGIONAL EFFECTS Tepest is altered by Lorinda's presence, creating the following effects: Closing the Borders. While in her domain, Mother Lorinda can close or open the borders to Tepest at will. While the borders are closed, a violent storm fills the Mists surrounding Tepest. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there must succeed on a DC 15 Strength saving throw or take 2d8 Lightning damage and be pushed up to 30 feet straight toward Tepest. A creature makes this save only once per turn. Mother's Watchers. Creatures that have sacrificed an eye to Mother can take a Magic action and pray to her. When they do so, Lorinda can perceive through the creature's senses as well as her own until the end of the creature's next turn. When perceiving through a creature's senses, Lorinda benefits from any special senses it has ‘Sun and Storms. While in Tepest, Lorinda can cast Control Weather, requiring no Material components and using the same spellcasting ability as Spelicasting. When casting the spell this way, Lorinda can control the weather within 5 miles of herself, regardless of whether she is inside or outside. If Lorinda dies, these effects end immediately. MOTHER LorinpDAé's TALE The sisters Lorinda, Laveeda, and Leticia Mindefisk lived desperate lives at the wooded edge of a terrible land. Their brutish family claimed the girls were unnatural fey in disguise and forced them to toil constantly. After years of harsh treatment from their cruel father and brothers, the hopeless sisters snapped and killed an abusive traveler during a chance encounter. They butchered the body and served the remains to their unwitting family. Thus began a cycle of murder and cannibalism that persisted for years, The Mindefisk sisters’ crimes were finally revealed when they all fell in love with a charming stranger. They squabbled, and unwilling to relent or share, they murdered the stranger in full view of their horrified family. With nothing left to lose, the sisters killed the rest of their family as the Mists rose around them, The horror of the Mindefisk sisters only grew in the Domains of Dread. Finding themselves in Tepest, the sisters discovered their new nature as hags. Over generations, mortals and fey alike came to know them as fickle miracle workers. Despairing families came to them often, willing to trade much for hexblood children born from the hags’ magic. Eventually, Lorinda grew fascinated by others’ pursuit of family. She entreated her sisters to help her create a child, but Laveeda and Leticia refused, loathing the idea of any one of them having greater happiness than the others. Defying her sisters, Lorinda assembled a monstrosity of animal parts and foul magic in secret. With her new daughter's help, the hag trapped her sisters inside their magic cauldron and stole their powers, Yet Lorinda's joy with her creation was short—the monster's magic quickly waned, and it rotted away. Ever since, Lorinda has sought to create a family of her own, producing countless monstrosities in experiments and approaching the villagers of Viktal in her guise as Mother. Over generations, this has given rise to the cult of the Mother in Viktal, but it has brought Lorinda no closer to attaining the lasting family connection she covets. MOTHER LorINDA's DOMAIN Tepest is altered by Lorinda’s presence, creating the following effects Closing the Borders. While in her domain, Mother Lorinda can close or open the borders to Tepest at will. While the borders are closed, a violent storm fills the Mists surrounding Tepest. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there must succeed on a DC 15 Strength saving throw or take 2d8 Lightning damage and be pushed up to 30 feet straight toward Tepest. A creature makes this save only once per turn. Mother's Watchers. Creatures that have sacrificed an eye to Mother can take a Magic action and pray to her. When they do so, Lorinda can perceive through the creature's senses as well as her own until the end of the creature's next turn. When perceiving through a creature's senses, Lorinda benefits from any special senses it has. Sun and Storms. While in Tepest, Lorinda can cast Control Weather, requiring no Material components and using the same spellcasting ability as Spelicasting. When casting the spell this way, Lorinda can control the weather within 5 miles of herself, regardless of whether she is inside or outside. If Lorinda dies, these effects end immediately. MOTHER Lorinpa's LAIR Ranging across the domain is Mother Lorinda's home, the Gurgyl. This towerlike structure of thorns, wicker, and giants’ bones walks on three skeletal dragon legs. THE GURGYL The villagers of Viktal know the Gurgyl serves their god in some way, but sighting it portends bad luck. The site has the following features:

Page 175

Kitchen-Laboratory. The Darklord concocts foul creation magic in this central chamber. Nursery-Oubliette. This room is home to whichever monster Lorinda calls her daughter, Laoirse. Old Cauldron. Lorinda's hag sisters languish in this plugged, oversize pot. ADVENTURES IN TEPEST Adventures set in Tepest feature rural cults, deceptive fey, and dread of nature's mysteries. Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain, Celebrants parade from Viktal to Mother's Sight to honor their god on the day of the Tithe # Sylvain Sarraith HORROR IN TEPEST Adventures in Tepest often include the following elements STRANGENESS IN VIKTAL The locals seem welcoming and vigorous in their worship of the nature deity, Mother. The village leader, Wyan, guides them in their faith, assisted by elders called Mother's Minders—all of whom are missing their left eye. Residents invite visitors to stay at the village's only inn, the Fisher's Rest, free of charge for as long as they please. They encourage new arrivals to linger and participate in their upcoming festival, called the Tithe (detailed later in this section) MorHer's CHILDREN Multiple times a year, Lorinda goes through a cycle of trying to magically create a new child. She calls this monstrous being Laoirse and dotes on it for a time. She believes that sacrifices to Laoirse during the Tithe can extend her child's life, but inevitably, the monster's magic fades, and it becomes mud and bones once more. For weeks afterward, Lorinda remains inconsolable, and violent storms and strange disasters plague Tepest. Lorinda's children take varied forms. To create such a monster, choose a stat block appropriate to challenge your characters, then consider how that creature might appear if it were made from different parts. For example, a Minotaur of Baphomet or Werewolf might be a bipedal mixture of ram, stag, and wolf parts, while a Shambling Mound might be a person made of wicker. These descriptions should seem outlandish but reflective of the creature's traits and actions. And no matter how bizarre her children are, Lorinda loves them all the same. TEPEST CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Tepest often involve Viktal’s cult and the dangerous spirits of the wilds. A campaign here might follow this outline:

Page 176

Levels 1-2. Characters find themselves welcomed in Viktal and encouraged to take part in the Tithe (see the adventure below). At the rite's conclusion, the villagers try to sacrifice one or more characters to one of Mother's children. The characters defeat the monster, earning the enmity of both the villagers and Mother Lorinda. Level 3-5, Fey creatures or druids who have been victims of Lorinda aid the characters; they reveal that she is no god, but a powerful hag who wanders the land in a walking tower called the Gurgyl. They send the group on a quest to learn from a mystical site or elder forest spirit how to infiltrate the hag's lair. Levels 6-8. After learning how to infiltrate the Gurgyl, the characters locate and break into Lorinda’s lair, finding some of the hag's bizarre creations and her diminished, imprisoned sisters. When they emerge from the mobile fortress, they find themselves near Viktal, where Lorinda confronts them, backed by her fanatical followers. Levels 9+. If the group defeats Lorinda, the land spirals into chaos as fey from Mount Arak run wild, The characters might become involved in a power struggle between the good and evil fey of Arak. Lorinda's return might drive the fey back—but the Darklord will help only if someone becomes her new child. THE TITHE Adventure for Level 2 Characters Situation, The characters participate in a religious celebration, at the end of which they are poised to become sacrifices. Hook. The human leader of Viktal, Wyan (Medium, Neutral Evil Priest) invites the characters to participate in the Tithe, a rite honoring their god, Mother. Map 2.33: Mother's Sight Francesca Baerald # Francesca Baerald ENCOUNTERS This adventure begins the day of the Tithe, after Friendly villagers from Viktal (Commoners) lead the characters to Mother's Sight. It uses map 2.33 and consists of these encounters. Tithe Competitions. Wyan directs the assemblage in a prayer to Mother, and the day of games begins. Characters each can play three games, participating in each selected game once. During each game, a character makes the ability check listed on the table below three times. Those who succeed on the check two or more times win the game; If they fail a villager wins. Tithe Game Check Crooked Joust. Participants use stilts strapped to their arms and legs to topple one another. Strength (Athletics) Powrie Chase. Pursuers play tag in while wearing tattered red cloaks. Dexterity (Stealth) Lost Siblings. Blindfolded participants identify other players by touching their hair. Intelligence (Investigation) Gossamer Glutton. Whoever eats the most live moths wins, Constitution Never Naughty. Participants flatter elderly villagers, who decide who wins and who gets paddled. Charisma (Persuasion) Characters who don't play a game might socialize and see some benign oddity extrapolated from the details presented earlier in this section Competition Winners. The day's games draw to a close at dusk. Wyan names a character who won two or more games as Mother's Favorite and crowns them with a garland of white camellias. If no character won this many games, a human villager named Lorelei (Medium Commoner) wins the crown and invites them to sit with her at the head table during the feast.

Page 177

Feast, Wyan begins the feast with another prayer as the Mother's Minders light a grand bonfire within a tall scaffold. During the feast, villagers encourage the characters eat and drink to excess. A character who does so must succeed on a DC 10 Constitution saving throw or gain the Poisoned condition for the next 6 hours. The Sacrifice. At midnight, standing before the scaffold (see map 2.33), Wyan announces, "Now we prove our undying love for Mother and beg her for another season of bounty. Bring forth our sacrifice!" Mother's Favorite receives a bowl of food and tiny crafts and is instructed to take it to the bonfire, If Lorelei is Mother's Favorite, she obeys and approaches the bonfire, If a character is Mother's Favorite, they choose whether to approach. Regardless, 2 rounds later, a biped with wolflike features and stag's horns (use the Minotaur of Baphomet stat block) emerges from the field nearest Mother's Favorite. This is one of Lorinda's monstrous children. It fights Mother's Favorite (and anyone else who damages it) to the death. During combat, the villagers raise their voices in a hymn but stay out of the conflict. Lorelei smiles and doesn't defend herself if attacked, Conclusion. if the monster dies, Wyan and the villagers are horrified that the Tithe went wrong, They turn Hostile, blaming the characters and demanding they leave, Mother Lorinda learns of this and seeks revenge. If the monster wasn't killed, the villagers remain Friendly, but their true nature becomes clear, Mother Lorinda also takes an interest in the characters, potentially seeking to adopt them as her new children. Regardless of how the Tithe concludes, the characters gain 200 XP. VALACHAN Domain of the Hunter Darklord: Chakuna Genres: Gothic horror and slasher horror Hallmarks: Deadly predators, death traps, hostile wilderness, survival games Mist Talismans: Displacer beast skin, poisonous flower blossom, rusty foot trap Aligned Tarokka Card: Shepherd Opposed Tarokka Card: Darklord # Pam Wishbow # Pam Wishbow In the wild domain of Valachan, only the deadliest predators survive. Bestial hunters, carnivorous plants, and ravenous monsters stalk the dense rainforests, but the land's greatest threat is its Darklord: the devious, undying hunter Chakuna Chakuna stalks the jungle, testing herself against the domain's deadliest beasts. Yet little delights her more than discovering strangers to her realm-visitors who soon realize they've become the Darklord’s newest quarry. Chakuna forces her victims to participate in the Trial of Hearts, a race through the wilderness to remote sanctuaries. Participants face deadly traps and all the dangers of the wilds, including Chakuna herself. Those who reach the goal at the end of the trial are permitted to leave Valachan. Those who don't reach it die, either due to the deadly wilderness or at the hands of Chakuna herself.

Page 178

DOMAIN FEATURES Valachan is known for the following features: Displacer Beast Packs. Packs of Displacer Beasts roam the jungle, led by Yana, a preternaturally large and cunning displacer beast that serves Chakuna, Natural Hunters. Wild plants and creatures in Valachan usually seem hostile toward strangers. Most creatures here are carnivorous even if elsewhere they arent. Trial of Hearts, The deadly Trial of Hearts is a battle royale that starts on the night of the full moon. Chakuna chooses participants. Wilderness Domain. Valachan is covered by a fantastically dangerous rainforest. Visitors face constant perils, but the domain's residents flourish. LOCATIONS IN VALACHAN Valachan is lush and wild, Rare creatures and plants reign here, as do villages of people who understand and thrive in the rainforest. No roads pass through the land, but ships from other domains sometimes wreck on these shores. The settlement of Oselo and the Pantara Lodge are described below, and other notable locations are summarized on the Valachan Locations table, OSELO The former home of Darklord Chakuna, Oselo is a prosperous village that welcomes visitors, When night falls, the residents change, shifting from people into panthers, ocelots, tigers, and other hunting cats, These shape-shifters hunt animals for food and do battle with other peoples only to defend their home. Most fear Chakuna and want no involvement with the Trial of Hearts, fearing the Darklord’s wrath, However, in rare instances, Oselo's shape-shifters might be convinced to guide, hide, or otherwise aid participants in the Trial, This displeases Chakuna, but she respects the decisions of her people's elders. The villagers might adopt strangers deemed worthy, but outsiders first must prove themselves in an initiation ceremony focused on surviving unarmed in the forest for a week. PANTARA LODGE The Pantara Lodge is a series of thatched structures strung together high in the forest canopy, suspended above the overgrown ruins of a burned castle, This is Chakuna's home. Its interconnected treehouses include a great hall, the Darklord's personal quarters, an armory, an infirmary, and several guest lodges. The Darklord's Displacer Beast and Panther allies prow/ the trees amid the lodge's structures and can enter them freely. » ARCACHAN ese Gai Map alachan Player Version f Jared Blando # Jared Blando Valachan Locations Location Description Council of the Whip Within this cavern, a sapient Giant Spider presides over representatives of countless insect species, Those seeking Spider insect allies might entreat these councilors for aid. Few can elude her cold cunning, deadly traps, and packs of feline allies, Chakuna is no mere murderer. She hunts because she must to protect the people of Oselo from a more terrible fate, To that end, she prefers to target strangers in her land, forcing them to participate in the Trial of Hearts, She relishes the chance to hunt the proud and disrespectful, as well as the skilled and unpredictable. Chakuna knows no mercy, though, and even those she comes to respect eventually face her blade. She spares

REGIONAL EFFECTS Valachan is altered by Chakuna's presence, creating the following effects. ‘Closing the Borders. While in her domain, Chakuna can close or open the borders to Valachan at will. While the borders are closed, shadowy beasts skulk or soar amid the Mists surrounding Valachan. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 1410 Piercing damage Heightened Senses. Beasts and Monstrosities have Advantage on Wisdom checks. Whispering Shadows. Humanoids subtract 1d8 from any ability checks they make when they take the Hide action. If Chakuna dies, these effects end immediately. CHAKUNA'S TALE For untold generations, Darklord Urik von Kharkov ruled Valachan from Castle Pantara and hunted the people of Valachan for sport. Yearly, the Darklord's hunts grew in carnage, with his favorite prey being the shape-shifters of Oselo, Von Kharkov viewed himself as the domain's greatest hunter, a title he confirmed every time he slaughtered one of Oselo's supernatural warriors. Chakuna was a werepanther warrior of Oselo and, like most of her people, was a skillful hunter. Seeing that von Kharkov eventually would slay her entire village, she swore to save Oselo, Realizing it would take a monster to slay a monster, she left her people and sought the deepest secrets of her deadly homeland. When she returned, she volunteered to let von Kharkov hunt her. During the murderous game that followed, Chakuna burned Castle Pantara and took the former Darklord's head. Valachan is no mundane land, though. A Domain of Dread demands a Darklord, and Valachan will be ruled only by its greatest hunter. Refusing to let von Kharkov return, Chakuna made a pact with the domain itself. Within the depths of the mazelike Yaguara Mountains, Chakuna ripped her own heart from her chest and consumed the heart of Urik von Kharkov. Doing so, she stole von Kharkov's powers. Yet to retain them, she must feed the land constantly with the blood of the hunted. If she doesnt, the plants and animals of Valachan grow murderous and how! for von Kharkov's return. So Chakuna continues a cycle of bloodshed, embracing the violence of her peoples’ onetime oppressor to ensure her bitter domination of her vicious realm. CHAKUNA's DOMAIN Valachan is altered by Chakuna’s presence, creating the following effects. Closing the Borders. While in her domain, Chakuna can close or open the borders to Valachan at will. While the borders are closed, shadowy beasts skulk or soar amid the Mists surrounding Valachan. A creature that enters the Mists for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 1410 Piercing damage. Heightened Senses, Beasts and Monstrosities have Advantage on Wisdom checks Whispering Shadows, Humanoids subtract 148 from any ability checks they make when they take the Hide action, If Chakuna dies, these effects end immediately. CHAKUNAS LAIR Built above the overgrown ruins of von Kharkov's Castle Pantara, the Pantara Lodge is a maze of rope bridges, mighty boughs, and structures built among the branches of gigantic trees, Chakuna leaves those who die in the Trial of Hearts without demonstrating skill or honor dangling from the branches of her home. PANTARA LODGE The Pantara Lodge rises 80 feet above the rainforest floor. The site is depicted on map 2.35 and has the following features: Chakuna's Chambers. In her quarters, Chakuna studies lore from other realms, fruitlessly seeking ways to placate her domain without further bloodshed. Secret doors hidden here lead to her meditation chamber and a room where the undying head of Urik von Kharkov spits curses and plots his return, Great Hall and Banquet Room. Chakuna welcomes guests in the lodge's great hall. Prior to the Trial of Hearts every moon, she hosts a lavish meal for her prey. ‘Trophy Gallery. The trophy gallery holds the skulls of deadly beasts, the weapons of those who failed to murder Chakuna, and the stuffed corpses of the Darklord’s rivals—animals and people alike. ADVENTURES IN VALACHAN

Page 183

Adventures set in Valachan involve merciless wilderness, predatory animals, and deadly hunts. Use this section to develop horror adventures in this domain. Lethal traps and hidden snares threaten participants in the Trial of Hearts # Syleain Sarath HORROR IN VALACHAN Adventures in Valachan often include the following elements. TRIAL OF Hearts RUL Visitors to Valachan likely are forced to participate in the Trial of Hearts, a dangerous game where the domain's Darklord hunts them, On the night of the full moon, Chakuna selects those she considers worthy to participate. At the Pantara Lodge, she shows her guests every courtesy, provides them with a map of the domain, and sets out these rules for the trial: + Participants can divide into small groups or go it alone. + Once groups are established at the trials start, alliances between contenders aren't permitted, + Participants must reach one of two shrines: either on Kiru Island or between the lakes called the Scars atop Anquara Plateau + Participants can leave the Pantara Lodge at dawn. The following dusk, Chakuna pursues the contenders with hunting partners of her choice, attempting to slay any participants she encounters outside a shrine. + Only one person or group can occupy a shrine. + Participants can kill one another for any reason—particularly to take a shrine, + The trial doesnt stop for any reason until Chakuna arrives at both shrines and acknowledges any winners there. + The Darklord's servants escort winners to the village of Shuaran, and from there, out of Valachan. + Losers rot where they fall, Rule breakers are killed. Chakuna wants the trial to be challenging. If she catches a competitor too soon, she might release them if they sacrifice an ally, a limb, or a valuable weapon. Anyone who dies in the trial feeds the domain's bloodlust—even those who fall to a threat other than the Darklord herself.

Page 184

‘VALACHAN EXPLORATION Valachan is a dense and deadly wilderness. When creating adventures here, refer to chapter 2 of the Dungeon Master's Guide for details on travel and journeys with stages. Characters use the rules in the "Travel Pace" section of the Player's Handbook. Valachan's forests prevent a party from traveling at faster than a Normal pace, and their density makes them Difficult Terrain, reducing travel pace by half. This means most characters can move through the domain at a Normal pace of 11% miles per hour and 12 miles per day. Flying. Difficult Terrain slows flying creatures in Valachan, due to the strong winds above the forest canopy. Flying creatures are also easy to spot, attracting the attention of Chimeras, Pteranodons, and condor-like Rocs. Chakuna might stipulate that flight is off-limits during the Trial of Hearts. If she does, the Mists aid in enforcing this rule. Navigation, It's easy to get lost in Valachan's jungles. Unless they use gorges and waterways for guidance, characters must make DC 15 Wisdom (Survival) checks to avoid getting lost. Those who fail spend 1d4 hours wandering and risk encountering deadly creatures (see "Trial of Hearts’ for suggestions). Characters should make this check once during each stage of a journey, or after dramatic or disorienting encounters, Trial Route: Kiru Island. An approximately 20-mile route from the Pantara Lodge to Kiru Island runs along the gorge south of the lodge to the Eirubamba River, then along the coast to Kiru Island, At a Normal pace, this route takes a day and a half (13 hours) to complete. Roughly 10 miles of this route follows the river, Characters who use a raft can move at Fast pace along the river, Rapids make the river Difficult Terrain, but even so, moving at this pace reduces the journey to just over a day (11 hours), Tria} Route: The Scars. Participants in the Trial of Hearts who travel from the Pantara Lodge to the Scars on Anquara Plateau have a route of approximately 20 miles if they follow the Shadowtaker Lakes east to the river called Killer's Road, then ascend Anquara Plateau. Characters can follow most of this route at a Normal pace, but 2 miles involve climbing into the mountains—a Slow pace through Difficult Terrain, reducing movement to 1 mile per hour. This route takes over a day and a half (14 hours) to complete. Displacer beasts and other deadly feline hunters stalk the bridges of the Pantara Lodge # Bastien Grivet VVALACHAN CAMPAIGN ARC Campaigns set in Valachan often feature the Trial of Hearts and Chakuna’s connection to her domain. A campaign here might follow this outline: Levels 1-3. Consider starting this campaign in another domain or world, During their travels, the characters become shipwrecked, Level 4-6. The characters wash up on the shores of Valachan. They must find ways to survive the rainforest and fend off a pack of Displacer Beasts that stalks them. They eventually find shelter in Oselo or Shuaran. Levels 7-9, Having heard of their exploits, Chakuna invites the characters to the Pantara Lodge, offering to help them leave if they participate in the Trial of Hearts (see the adventure below). Before the trial ensues, the characters might learn secrets from the head of Urik von Kharkov. Levels 10+. if the characters survive the trial, Chakuna helps them leave the domain. As they characters travel out of Valachan, locals from Shuaran or Oselo beg the characters to stay and slay Chakuna. Should the characters kill Chakuna, chaos erupts and Urik von Kharkov strives to. retake power in Chakuna's absence

TRIAL OF HEARTS Adventure for Level 8 Characters Situation. To escape Valachan, the characters must participate in the Trial of Hearts. Hook. The hunter Chakuna (see earlier in this chapter) invites characters to Pantara Lodge to discuss helping them leave the domain. Map 2.35: Pantara Lodge Player Version # Sean Mocdonald # Sean Macdonald ENCOUNTERS The adventure uses map 2.35 and consists of these encounters. Pantara Lodge. Chakuna seems welcoming and polite, inviting characters to relax before a feast where she'll explain how to leave Valachan, Note the Indifferent Displacer Beasts that prow! the lodge—Chakuna's guards and hunting companions. The characters receive a comfortable guest room where they can rest before dinner. Chakuna’s Guests. The characters aren't Chakuna’s only quests. There are three other groups: Lizardfolk, Tolocwa (Lizardfolk Sovereign) and three lizardfolk (Medium Warrior Veterans) are their tribe's best hunters, Pirates, The human Rakengel (Medium Pirate Captain) and three Pirates from his crew were recently shipwrecked. Thieves, Aian, Citana, and Lesar are Weretigers from Oselo who were caught stealing a rare herb, Trial Rules. After Chakuna’s guests have all gathered for a meal of vegetarian dishes and mineral water, Chakuna does the following + Explains the trial, outlining the rules from the “Trial of Hearts Rules" section, + Insists this is the only way to leave Valachan, + Notes that the group could attack her, but they would face her Displacer Beast allies. If they want to fight, they should wait for the trial, The pirate group speaks up in protest, but growls from Chakuna's allies silence them. The Darklord answers questions, then encourages her guests to rest, as the trial starts at dawn. Those who leave before then violate the rules. Cheating. Chakuna catches anyone who tries to leave the lodge early or otherwise cheat. She encourages them to obey the rules and calls on six Displacer Beasts to enforce them. She calls the monsters off before anyone dies. Choosing a Route, Have the characters choose a shrine as their destination and determine their route (see "Valachan Exploration") Trial Encounters, The trial is divided into three phases, including a night camping in the jungle, As you narrate the characters’ journey through the wilderness, roll on the following table for an encounter that happens during each of the first two phases and another that occurs while camping, Jungle Events 148 Characters Encounter... 1 ‘An ambush by the thieves (see above). 2 Chakuna, who runs past covered in blood; then four Displacer Beasts attack. r Flooding or rock falls. A character must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom (Survival) check or 2 hours are added to the journey. 4 Four Swarms of Ravenous Insects.* 5 Three Zombie Plague Spreaders* or a Relentless Slasher that survived a previous trial. 6 ‘A Hydra or two Shambling Mounds. 7 A Tyrannosaurus Rex or two Giant Crocodiles. 8 ‘A murdered member of another group. Roll a die; if you roll an even number, the corpse has a Potion of Healing. *Detailed in chapter 5 Chakuna During the Trial. Chakuna might appear and menace characters, but don't track her travels or have her fight the characters directly, Kiru isiand Event. During the last phase of their journey, groups traveling to Kiru Island must find a way to cross Sangui Cove. While crossing, they are attacked by a Giant Shark and a Giant Crocodile.

Page 186

‘Scars Event. During the last phase of their journey, groups traveling to the Scars must climb 120 feet up Anquara Plateau. While they do, a Giant Ape attacks them from above. Trial Shrine. At both destinations, the trial shrine is a wooden platform bearing a statue of a panther. It hangs at the end of a 200-foot-long rope bridge suspended 60 feet over a spiked pit. Those who fall into the pit take 21 (6d6) Bludgeoning damage plus 36 (8d8) Piercing damage. Rival. f the characters reached the shrine in less than 15 hours, they are the first ones there. After 244 hours, the pirate group arrives at the Kiru Island shrine, and the lizardfolk group arrives at the Scars shrine. Both groups are missing any members who died in previous encounters, but they're Hostile and fight the party. If the characters took longer than 15 hours, this rival group arrives first. Conciusion. An hour after the group contends with its rivals, Chakuna arrives. If the characters followed the rules of the Trial of Hearts, she pronounces them victors and upholds her end of the bargain—helping them leave Valachan. If they broke the trials rules in even the slightest way, Chakuna knows it and fights them. In either case, each character earns a bonus 2,100 XP, OTHER DOMAINS OF DREAD None can say how many domains drift through the Mists. This section hints at the multitudes of additional Domains of Dread. Detail and explore them in your adventures, or use them as examples when creating your own domains using chapter 4. THE AGENCY Darklord: Flimira Vhage Hallmarks: Detective work, memory loss Everything inside the office of the Agency is a monotone gray. Anyone who passes the frosted glass door into this single-room domain is expected by Flimira "Flintlock" Vhage, the detective agency's owner. From here, Flimira collects cryptic correspondence relating to mysteries across the Domains of Dread. She enlists agents to investigate these cases, then report back to her. However, she never reveals her own past as a detectiveturned-criminal, her involvement in every case her agency investigates, or that the Agency exists within her mind BLUETSPUR Darklord: The God-Brain Hallmarks: Abductions, false memories, illithids The entity that would become the God-Brain of Bluetspur was an illithid elder brain whose existence-violating experiments revealed a malignant truth of the multiverse. This secret manifested in the elder brain's mind as an alien disease. Terrified of learning this pernicious, devouring knowledge themselves, the other illithid elder brains banished their peer from existence. In actuality, the Dark Powers claimed the afflicted elder brain and granted it an alien domain. Now called the God-Brain by the mind flayers and stranger entities that serve it, this elder brain pursues radical methods of saving itself from the thoughts destroying it. ts servants claim trespassers and beings from other domains, violating their minds with their bizarre experiments. CARNIVAL Darklords: Isolde and Nepenthe Hallmarks: Bargains, misfits, wandering exiles The Carnival is a wandering domain that appears within other Domains of Dread. Before the Carnival appears, mysterious fliers blow into communities near where it's about to arrive. When it arrives, the Carnival sets up a surreal wonderland of entertainment and oddities staffed by performers, outcasts, and cursed individuals from countless lands. Attractions include grand shows at the big top, sideshow performances, and the Hall of Horrors, The Carnival's mysterious leader is Isolde, a good but wrathful warrior of the Feywild who's cursed to lead the Carnival even as she hunts an elusive foe across the Domains of Dread. She carries the bladelike Nepenthe, a weapon that hungers for blood. Although driven by vengeance and painful memories, Isolde protects the Carnival at all costs. CyreE 1313, THE MOURNING RAIL

Page 187

Map 2.36: Cyre 1313 Lightning Rail Cart Player Version # Stacey Allan & Wiliam Doyle # Stacey Man & Wiliam Doyle Darklord: The Last Passenger Hallmarks: Escape from disaster, lightning rail The disaster known as the Mourning numbers among the greatest tragedies to befall the world of Eberron—a mysterious calamity that killed nearly everyone in the land of Cyre. in the nation's capital of Metrol, some citizens foresaw the coming devastation and sought to escape on lightning rails: elemental-powered engines capable of pulling trains of passenger carts. As scared innocents packed Metro!’s last lightning rail, known as Cyre 1313, the evacuation was delayed at the demand of a late-arriving VIP. Hundreds were forced from passenger carts to admit and maintain the secrecy of this last passenger and their retinue. When the lightning rail finally departed, it was too late-the Mourning overtook the train and its escapees. But even as it did so, the Mists claimed Cyre 1313 and all aboard it. Now, the last lightning rail from Metro! hurtles through the Mists as a traveling domain. Those on board fear the disaster pursuing them, the mysterious passenger seated in the train's foremost cart, and the necrotic energy now infusing the engine's elemental spirit. Yet none of the passengers realizes that their endless quest for escape is pointless, as Cyre 1313 carries only the dead, FORLORN Darklord: Tristen ApBlanc Hallmarks: Life and death, strange invention Tristen ApBlanc was born amid tragedy, the son of a vampire father and a human noble. Fearful villagers murdered his parents, leaving the boy to be adopted by druids. But during his teenage years, his dhampir nature revealed itself. When the druids discovered the youth's hunger for blood, they cast him out, Tristen grew enraged and slaughtered them, draining them all. The sacred waters of the druids’ rituals had infused their blood, though, making it poison to the dhampir. Tristen died with his adopted family, but as the Mists closed in around their sacred stone circle, he rose ‘as a ghost.

Page 188

Unexpectedly, with the dawn, the Darklord’s dhampir body was restored. Now, he lives by day, a perpetually young, charming, invention-obsessed dhampir dwelling in Castle Tristenoira, the smoking fortress his goblin servants built over the druid circle of his onetime family. At night, though, the dhampir dies a painful death, then rises in his spectral form to scour all that is green and vibrant from his land. GHASTRIA Darklord: Stezen d'Polarno Hallmarks: Cursed art, dour population Anotorious hedonist, Marquis Stezen d'Polarno enjoyed popularity among his noble peers but craved immortality. A mysterious artist offered him eternity by painting Stezen's portrait on a magical canvas. But the artist didn't mention that the painting would strip Stezen of his love of life and natural charm. Despite his dulled existence, the marquis enjoys one reprieve: Once every season, when he shows his portrait to an audience, it consumes their souls and refreshes his thrill for life. Stezen's artistic predations captured the attention of the Dark Powers, which drew his lands into the Mists, Now, Ghastria is a fertile island that, like its lord, lacks an essential vim—except for once per season, when vigor fleetingly returns. G'HENNA Darklord: Yagno Petrovna Hallmarks: Corrupt theocracy, false deity Born of a Barovian family, Yagno Petrovna went missing on the slopes of Mount Ghakis as a youth. As a violent storm rose, he took shelter in a mysterious ruin and was found weeks later wandering the hills, babbling about an amber idol and the god he'd discovered: Zhakata the Provider and the Devourer. His family sought to help him, but when they learned Yagno had been secretly sacrificing people to his fictitious god, they chased him into the Mists. When he emerged, the domain of G'henna sprawled before him. Life is hard in G'henna, a rocky land home to fierce, ravenous animals. The domain's starving people worship the bestial god Zhakata and regularly travel to the cathedral-city of Zhukar. There, they offer their crops in sacrifice and hear Zhakata's will through the revered prophet Yagno Petrovna, I'CaTH Darklord: Tsien Chiang Hallmarks: Labyrinth, inescapable dreamworld Inher youth, Tsien Chiang accidentally slew her gold dragon mentor to create a magic, wish-granting bell capable of driving invaders from her homeland. Her scheme worked, and the people made Chiang their queen. Over time, though, the people bristled under Chiang's rule and rose up in revolt, Slaughter and loss on both sides led Chiang to ring her magic bell again, but this time the bell cracked, and the Mists spilled forth. When they faded, Tsien Chiang was the ruler of Cath, a labyrinthine, nightmare city where the people sleep endlessly. In their dreams, they toil forever in a fantasy version of the city, working to maintain the perfectionism of their harsh queen. INVIDIA Darklord: Gabrielle Aderre Hallmarks: Bad parents, possessed children Gabrielle Aderre is convinced that her son, Malocchio, is destined for greatness. From her estate outside the village of Karina, she employs an endless string of servants to provide Malocchio with the best possible upbringing. Inevitably, though, every servant flees or vanishes, either as a result of Gabrielle's unreasonable expectations or the deadly tricks and accidents that frequently occur around the child. Gabrielle isn't content to leave her son's grand destiny to chance, though. Using her precious bone spirit board, she calls on supernatural forces to guide and protect her son. Spirits, angels, fiends, and worse answer her summons—but as long as they chart Malocchio’s path to glory, the Darklord eagerly accepts their gifts, KALIDNAY

Page 189

Darklord: Thakok-An Hallmarks: Magical apocalypse, obsession The city of Kalidnay was once the seat of power of Kalid-Ma, one of the monstrous sorcerer kings of Athas. Thakok-An, the greatest of Kalid-Ma's enforcers, desired the sorcerer-king so greatly that when she learned of a plot against her master, she didn't reveal it, Instead, she performed a magical rite to grant Kalid-Ma even greater power, sacrificing her own family to do so. Her ritual went wrong, though, and life-draining magic washed over Kalidnay, destroying the city and draining life from nearly all its residents, Awaking amid the ruins, Thakok-An found Kalidnay surrounded by an endless sea of silt and mist. Worse, rather than being empowered, Kalid-Ma languished in a cursed torpor, the sorcerer-king's vast magic bleeding away. Now Thakok-An obsesses over her sleeping king. From Kalidnay’s survivors, she's created a new cult of servants. Following Thakok-An's wishes, these templars condemn trespassers in Kalidnay to ritual combat in the arena, where the lives of the slain sustain the secretly withering sorcererking. KEENING Darklord: Tristessa Hallmarks: Banshee, silent village The forbidding land of Keening is dominated by Mount Lament, at whose base lies the village of Anwrtyn. The people of Anwrtyn deafen themselves so they wor't hear the shrieking of the banshee Tristessa, a mournful spirit who roams Mount Lament and whose wail carries through the night. In life, Tristessa dwelled under Mount Arak in Tepest (detailed earlier in this book), but she was exiled for crimes against her people. In death, she endlessly seeks to reunite with the family slain by her misdeeds. The deadly shrieks of the banshee Tristessa echo through the mountainous domain of Keening f David Astruga KLORR Darklord: Klorr Hallmarks: Impending doom, surreal environment Klorr is the end of worlds. Here, shattered islands drift through a misty netherworld, caught in a swirling, clockwork spiral that ends at the unignorable, burning eye called Klorr. Thirteen stars orbit this sunlike sphere, one winking out every hour. Each time one of the stars dies, one of the domain's ruined islands is drawn into Klorr and consumed by flames. Afterward, each other island wrenches ahead, then halts, one hour closer

Page 190

to the same doom. When all thirteen stars have winked out, they return hourly to the sky. Those who dwell on the crumbling islands trapped in the domain count the hours until their end. Few know how they came to Klorr or when new islands get added to the cycle, only that the Mists closed in and doomed them. Amid the realm's surreal skies float the ruins of lost domains— among them, a city of skulls, a realm overrun by dogs, and an array of bloody, iron arenas. Echoes of domains that exist elsewhere in the Domains of Dread also drift here, including at least one duplicate of Castle Ravenloft. Those cast away amid this orderly apocalypse grow ever more desperate to defy the doomsday clock and the will of a hidden Darklord, the obsessed clockmaker named Klorr. MARKOVIA Darklord: Frantisek Markov Hallmarks: Depraved science, sapient animals Dr. Frantisek Markov is a genius~but less so than yesterday. His Markov Formula grants him unparalleled intellect, but it insidiously steals more than it gives, making him increasingly dull-witted and bestial in form. Despairing, the doctor fled to a tropical island he dubbed Markovia, where he tests new versions of his formula on the local fauna in hopes of recovering his waning genius. As a result of these tests, animals across Markovia now possess sapience and have been deluded into worshipping Markov as their god. NIGHTMARE LANDS Darklord: The Nightmare Court Hallmarks: Nightmares, recurring dreams ‘Any who sleep might become forever trapped in the Nightmare Lands, a phantasmagoric realm whose features shift endlessly. Those who visit and escape speak of malicious wildernesses; the empty city called Nod; and uncountable drifting spheres, each containing a stranger's unending nightmare, Insidious entities called the Nightmare Court rule the domain. No one knows how many members make up the court, but they include the tragically graceful Ghost Dancer, the tomb-bound Hypnos, the witch Mullonga, the trickster Morpheus, and the embodiment of terror known as the Nightmare Man. These beings, artisans of nightmares, visit terrors on any whose sleeping minds brush against the domain. The Nightmare Court's members share one commonality: All are the living nightmares of Caroline Dinwiddy, a potent psychic who has repressed memories of her own heartless deeds. These memories torment her sleeping mind, creating the Nightmare Court. Deep within the City of Nod, inside a warped reimagining of the clinic where she once worked, Caroline sleeps without waking, refusing to face the terrors her dreams unleash on innocents across the Domains of Dread and beyond. NIRANJAN Darklord: Sarthak Hallmarks: Asceticism, brainwashing, shadows The Ashram of Niranjan is a vibrant vihara, or monastery, on a misty chain of islands. Here the elderly scholar Niranjan leads ascetics in practicing Ramsana, a way of life prioritizing nonattachment to the material world. In truth, Niranjan is Sarthak, a wicked bronze dragon who sends agents into the Mists bearing his philosophical writings. These works promise escape and peace to any who adopt their teachings and search the Mists for their source. Those who come to the ashram must divest themselves of worldly goods, which become part of Sarthak's hidden hoard. Sarthak then helps his victims enter a trance that causes their souls to slip away from their bodies. Sarthak consumes these souls and replaces them with shadows, leaving the victims’ bodies under his control. Nova VAASA Darklord: Myar Hiregaard Hallmarks: Nomadic riders, transformation ‘An unparalleled warrior, Myar Hiregaard united the nomadic tribes of the vast plains of Nova Vaasa. But, while respected as a soldier, Hiregaard made a poor peacetime leader. When brutal games could no longer keep her interest, she incited hostilities between two of her vassal tribes, then led her own forces to crush them. Subtly, she did this again and again.

Page 191

After Hiregaard's greatest massacre, the Mists enfolded all of Nova Vaasa, splitting the Darklord's personality in two when they did. Now she rules her people with strict fairness—but when her bloodlust is piqued, she transforms into the raging knight called Malkan and sows discord across the plains. ODAIRE Darklord: Maligno Hallmarks: Evil toys, village of children The toymaker Giuseppe had his wish for a family granted when his creation, the marionette Figlio, came to life. A proud father, the toymaker presented his son to all the people of his village, Odaire. The local children loved Figlio, but their skeptical parents said the marionette was nothing but a toy. Over time, this doubt enraged Figlio, and the marionette convinced Giuseppe to craft siblings for him. Then, when the time was right, Giuseppe's creations did away with all the adults in Odaire, Claimed by the Mists, Odaire is a village populated only by children and ruled by Figlio the Carrionette (see chapter 5), who now calls himself Maligno. oar Tor Shor Map 2.37: Odaire Toy Shop Player Version # NerdyMaps # NerdyMops RICHEMULOT Darklord: Jacqueline Renier Hallmarks: Martial law, plague, wererats ‘The once-beautiful cities of Richemulot crumble as season after season of the Gnawing Plague afflicts the domain, Countless commoners and aristocrats have died, causing rule to settle with the vain noble Jacqueline Renier, Unbeknownst to the people, Jacqueline is a wererat whose servants create and spread the plague. She loathes what her domain has become but refuses to cede power back to the people by ending the ongoing disaster. Jacqueline's obedient state police, the Casques Silencieux, enforce martial law and quarantines across the domain. RIDER'S BRIDGE Darklord: The Headless Rider Hallmarks: Haunted bridge, murderous legend Nearly every domain knows some version of the apparition called the Headless Rider. It appears as a mercenary in dark armor in Mordent, a ghostly cataphract in Har'Akir, and a mutated centaur in Lamordia. But in each incarnation, certain details remain true: the rider lacks its head, it appears on a prominent bridge, and it decapitates victims as it endlessly searches for its own head, Anyone who escapes the Headless Rider might find a different domain on the opposite side of the spirit's bridge. (See "Dullahan’ in chapter 5 for more details.) RISIBILOS Darklord: Doerdon Hallmarks: Misdirection, ventriloquism

Page 192

The Risibilos is a small music hall, similar to those found in any decent-sized city. Its lord, Doerdon, was once a king—one so thoroughly humorless that he forbade his subjects the privilege of laughter, on pain of death. He now finds himself cursed to entertain others—a task he is utterly unqualified for. Fortunately, the Mists delivered him a partner, a ventriloquist's dummy carved in the likeness of Strahd von Zarovich. The eerie dummy has a mind of its own, insisting it is the real Strahd and that the creature currently sitting on the throne of Castle Ravenloft is a mere impostor. It rages at the audience and makes audacious promises to any who will help it regain its station. Anyone with the slightest inkling of who Strahd is finds this hilarious. Their peals of laughter are agony to Doerdon's ears. SCAENA Darklord: Lemont Sediam Juste Hallmarks: Reality-manipulating theater Lemont Sediam Juste fancied himself a serious playwright, and he achieved popular, if not critical, acclaim in Dementlieu for his works of grisly hortor. But he craved respectability, and with his new play Apparitions, Lemont believed he would find it. The night of the premiere, when the audience signaled their boredom, the playwright was crestfallen, His supporters wanted blood, so he gave them what they craved. By the play's end, Lemont had joined the drama and brutally murdered every member of the cast while the crowd roared their approval. As the show ended, the playhouse broke from Dementlieu, and Scaena was formed. Comprising a single playhouse, the domain can create any reality Lemont desires on its stage. The Darklord’s immersive performances become somewhat predictable, though, as they always end in slaughter. SEA OF SORROWS Darklord: Pietra van Riese Hallmarks: Island domains, nautical horror The murderous pirate Pietra van Riese, captain of the Re/entless, had an unsavory reputation for attaching her captives to ropes and dragging them through the water until they drowned, She never removed the detritus of her victims, even though some returned to life as zombies. Rival captains ultimately sank the Relentless, but death couldn't keep Pietra, She awoke in the Sea of Sorrows, water in her lungs and sea creatures making their homes in her flesh. Her crew stirred with her, now fish-eaten corpses. When the captain sought to speak with them, her voice emerged from their mouths. The Relentless sails a domain that can overlap any body of water in any other domain. Some domains border the Sea of Sorrows, while others call these mysterious waters by other names. Those who venture into the Mists by ship might enter the sea's endless debris-choked expanse of eerie beasts and shifting islands, including the following: Blaustein. The island-fortress domain known as Blaustein once was ruled by the notorious Bluebeard, but his spectral wives overthrew him and now endlessly torment him. Dominia. The asylum of the vampire Dr. Daclaud Heinfroth rises up from the stormy island of Dominia. The asylum's patients are all dramatically different versions of Heinfroth, inspired by who he was at various points during his lengthy life. Isle of the Ravens. A storm of ravens surrounds this forested islet. Hidden amid the feathered gale, an impossibly tall tower reaches into the sky. The Isle of Ravens is home to the Lady of Ravens, and any who offend her join her ebon-winged flock. The Lighthouse. Otherworldly light shines from the Lighthouse, a twisted spur of bizarre fossils. Anyone who enters finds that instead of broadcasting a warning across the sea, the light calls down to what lurks in the pit hidden beneath it. Vigilant's Bluff. An undead paladin holds vigil atop Vigilant's Bluff. Weary travelers can find refuge here if they respect the paladin's faith. The bones of those who don't litter the surrounding coral. SOURAGNE Darklord: Anton Misroi Hallmarks: Imprisonment, swamp magic In society, Anton Misroi presented himself as an upstanding gentleman who served as a prison warden. But within the walls of the prison, he became a sadist who believed righteousness was on his side. When his torturous punishments finally drove the inmates to rise up, the bloody riot that ensued drew the attentions of the Dark Powers. During the uprising, Anton drowned in the swamps surrounding the prison. But he rose again soon after, an undead warden in search of inmates. Beyond Anton's prison, alligator-flled swamps cover the domain of Souragne right up to the sinking settlement of Port dElhour and Marais, d Tarascon, a village where above-ground crypts outnumber the residences of the living.

Page 193

: i In Souragne, the walking dead rise to terrorize the village of Marais d’Tarascon # Luca Bancone STAUNTON BLUFFS Darklord: Teresa Bleysmith Hallmarks: Endless warfare, repeating history Eerily faceless mercenary regiments sweep the countryside of Staunton Bluffs, Burning villages and killing helpless residents, they push ever eastward toward Castle Stonecrest, hereditary home of the Bleysmith family. Teresa Bleysmith, spurred by jealousy of her brother, Torrence, gave her family's foes the intelligence they needed to raid Staunton Bluffs. The attack was never supposed to go so far, but the duplicitous mercenaries stormed through the countryside to take Castle Stonecrest within a day. Teresa survived the attack—but when she surveyed the damage done and found her family dead by her own designs, she threw herself from the bluffs. Now she haunts her own domain, where she repeatedly relives the day of her betrayal TOVAG Darklord: Kas the Bloody Handed Hallmarks: Undead military dictatorship Notorious across the planes, the vampire Kas was once the champion of the lich Vecna. Wielding the mighty sword that bears his name, he betrayed his master, and the resulting battle supposedly destroyed them both. In truth, Vecna escaped and grew in power over ages and across. worlds, Kas, though, was claimed by the Mists, and in his wasteland domain of Tovag, he believes his war with Vecna rages on. Patrols of prisoner-soldiers under undead commanders scour the land, dragooning strangers to serve in Kas's armies and to manufacture bizarre war machines. When Kas deems the time right, he sends his forces into the Mists, believing that Vecna's realm lies just beyond. Invariably, those ‘troops never return, leaving the vampire to rage, rebuild his forces, and continue his search for the Sword of Kas (detailed in the Dungeon Master's Guide), which he considers his key to victory. At least once, the Dark Powers have released Kas from his domain to torment their onetime prisoner Vecna. Inevitably, when Kas fails to defeat his ancient rival, the Mists return him to his domain to rage for another era.

ZHERISIA Darklord: Sodo Hallmarks: Serial murderers, urban decay Each day, the city of Paridon in Zherisia erupts in riots over food scarcity, taxation, and the disappearance of dozens of citizens every night. At least one murderer stalks Paridon: the ancient doppelganger Sodo, which has impersonated so many people that it now finds it impossible to hold a form for more than a few days at a time. As Sodo's flesh runs like hot wax, it staves off dissolution by consuming the organs of humanoids. Paridon's streets serve as the Darklord's hunting ground, and those who enter the sewers risk attracting the notice of countless insectile horrors, including carrion stalkers (see chapter 5) and their monstrous Hive Queen.
